# Platform Theme Facts & Cross-Platform Property Mapping

---

### Conventions
#### Value sources
Every value in this document has a deterministic source. When an OS
provides no direct API for a property, the value is measured from rendered
controls and the source is annotated:

| Source tag          | Meaning                                                    |
|---------------------|------------------------------------------------------------|
| **(API)**           | System API, function, or queryable constant                |
| **(HIG)**           | Apple Human Interface Guidelines documentation             |
| **(Fluent)**        | Microsoft Fluent Design System / WinUI3 XAML resources     |
| **(Breeze src)**    | KDE Breeze style engine source (`breezemetrics.h`, etc.)   |
| **(Adwaita CSS)**   | GNOME libadwaita stylesheet values                         |
| **(measured)**      | Pixel-measured from rendered controls; OS version noted     |
| **(font metrics)**  | Computed from OpenType font metric tables (sTypo/hhea)     |
| **(WebKit)**        | Inferred from WebKit's native look implementation          |
| **(cross-project)** | No platform API; value from cross-project consensus (Qt, Firefox, etc.) |
| **(preset)**        | Value from our bundled preset TOML (originally measured)   |
| **(impl)**          | A material-web or Compose implementation constant, cited at its pinned line; a Breeze or libadwaita constant takes **(Breeze src)** or **(Adwaita CSS)** instead |
| **(app-defined)**   | The design system leaves the value to the application; no token or constant states it |
| **(none)**          | OS has no such concept; preset must supply a value         |

Properties annotated **(none)** are absent from the platform entirely — no API, no guideline, no rendered control to measure. The preset supplies a default for these.

#### Table symbols
| Symbol | Meaning                                                        |
|--------|----------------------------------------------------------------|
| `→`    | "returns / extract field" — the API on the left yields the value on the right. Example: `+systemFontOfSize:` → family means calling `+systemFontOfSize:` and reading its `family` property. |
| `←`    | "inherits from" — no widget-specific value; the property is taken from the referenced global default. Example: `← defaults.border.corner_radius` means use the global default corner radius. |
| L / D  | Light variant / Dark variant hex values.                       |
| ⚙      | Value can be changed by the user via a system setting (theme, accent, font choice, etc.). Inherited (`←`) properties are not marked — follow the chain to the source. |
| ↕      | Value scales with the system DPI / display scaling factor.     |
| ✅     | Verified and correct — confirmed against authoritative sources.|
| ❓     | Uncertain — low confidence; measured or inferred, no authoritative source found. |
| ❌     | Incorrect — disproved; see inline note for the correct information. |

#### Version-tagged values
A cell that tags its values with OS versions ("macOS 14: X · macOS 26: Y") states one value per version, and each value belongs to the preset of that version's generation (`macos-monterey`, and a later `macos-tahoe`), not to a usage context. The cell-reading rules (one number is stated, a per-context value only for a matching context; [unstated-sizes spec §1.4](archive/todo_v0.5.9_unstated-sizes-and-chrome-ux-spec.md)) therefore read per preset: a preset states the one number its own version carries, and a value tagged for another version says nothing about it. The size gate (`native-theme/src/presets/documented_sizes.rs`) keys its rows by preset for the same reason. In the macOS column (M2a Step 8, 2026-10-11) a cell's first value, untagged or tagged `macOS 14–15:`, is `macos-monterey`'s, which covers macOS 12–15 and was verified on 14 and 15; `macOS 26:` gives `macos-tahoe`'s, which covers 26 and was verified on 26.6.2 (the OS probe, [`evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/`](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/)): `same` where a source shows the value unchanged, the value where it differs, ❓ where no source gives it. An inheritance pointer (`←`) names a field of the model, not an OS value: an untagged pointer holds for both, and one tagged `macOS 14–15:` holds only for those versions.

---

## Chapter 1: What OSes Provide

Pure facts — every API, setting, and value available from each platform.
No abstractions, no invented structures.

### 1.1 macOS

#### 1.1.1 Fonts

**NSFont role-based class methods** (each returns family, pointSize, weight):

| Class method                              | Role             | Default result              |   |
|-------------------------------------------|------------------|-----------------------------|---|
| `+systemFontOfSize:`                      | Body text        | SF Pro, 13pt, Regular (400) | ✅ · macOS 26: same, 13pt, weight trait 0 ([probe 26 :29-33](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L29-L33); [HIG typography](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/design/human-interface-guidelines/typography.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| `+boldSystemFontOfSize:`                  | Bold body        | SF Pro, 13pt, Bold (700)    | ✅ · macOS 26: same, 13pt, weight trait 0.4 ([probe 26 :40-44](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L40-L44)) |
| `+monospacedSystemFontOfSize:weight:`     | Monospace        | SF Mono, 13pt, Regular (400)| ✅ · macOS 26: the same face as on 14.8.9 and 15.7.9, `.AppleSystemUIFontMonospaced` 13pt, weight trait 0 ([probe 26 :161-165](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L161-L165)); whether its family is "SF Mono" stays ❓ |
| `+titleBarFontOfSize:`                    | Window title bar | SF Pro, 13pt, Bold (700)    | ✅ the OS probe: `.AppleSystemUIFaceHeadline` 13pt on macOS 14.8.9, 15.7.9, 26.6.2 and 27.0.1, its `NSFontWeightTrait` 0.4 (Bold) on 15, 26 and 27 and 0.3 (Semibold) on 14.8.9 for the same face and metrics ([probe 14 :119-121](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L119-L121), [probe 15 :121](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-15/probe.toml#L121)) · macOS 26: same (the probe lines this cell cites) |
| `+menuFontOfSize:`                        | Menu items       | SF Pro, 13pt, Regular (400) | ✅ · the OS probe: 13pt, weight trait 0 (Regular) on 14.8.9, 15.7.9, 26.6.2 and 27.0.1 ([probe 14 :73-77](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L73-L77)) · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :73-77](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L73-L77)) |
| `+menuBarFontOfSize:`                     | Menu bar labels  | SF Pro, 13pt, Regular (400) | ❓ weight undocumented; no evidence of SemiBold; likely Regular like other font methods · the OS probe settles it: 13pt, weight trait 0 (Regular) on 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :84-88](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L84-L88)) · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :84-88](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L84-L88)) |
| `+toolTipsFontOfSize:`                    | Tooltip text     | SF Pro, 11pt, Regular (400) | ✅ the OS probe: 11pt on macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :98](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L98), [probe 15 :98](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-15/probe.toml#L98)), as the Leopard-era HIG states ("The small system font (11 point) is the default font for help tags", Apple's term for tooltips); Cocotron (10pt) and GNUstep (12pt) differ · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :98](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L98)) |
| `+paletteFontOfSize:`                     | Tool palettes    | SF Pro, 11pt, Regular (400) | ✅ the OS probe: 11pt on macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :131](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L131), [probe 15 :131](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-15/probe.toml#L131)); Cocotron and GNUstep default to 12pt, which AppKit does not return · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :131](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L131)) |
| `+controlContentFontOfSize:`              | Buttons/controls | SF Pro, 12pt, Regular (400) | ✅ the OS probe: 12pt for the default size (0) on macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :109](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L109), [probe 15 :109](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-15/probe.toml#L109)), not 13 · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :109](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L109)) |

| Property             | Value |   |
|----------------------|-------|---|
| `+systemFontSize`    | 13pt  | ✅ · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :16](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L16)) |
| `+smallSystemFontSize`| 11pt | ✅ · the OS probe: 11 on 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :17](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L17)) · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :17](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L17)) |
| `+labelFontSize`     | 10pt  | ✅ · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :18](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L18)) |

**NSFont.TextStyle** (macOS 11+; sizes are fixed — macOS does **not** support Dynamic Type): (macOS 26: same, [HIG typography](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/design/human-interface-guidelines/typography.json), read 2026-10-11)

| TextStyle      | Default size | Line height | Weight       |   |
|----------------|-------------|-------------|--------------|---|
| `.largeTitle`  | 26pt        | 32pt        | Regular (400)| ✅ Apple HIG JSON confirms 26pt; third-party impls (ViewKit 24pt, shaps80 30pt) were pre-macOS-11 approximations · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :186](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L186); [HIG typography](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/design/human-interface-guidelines/typography.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| `.title1`      | 22pt        | 26pt        | Regular (400)| ✅ Apple HIG · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :197](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L197)) |
| `.title2`      | 17pt        | 22pt        | Regular (400)| ✅ Apple HIG · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :208](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L208)) |
| `.title3`      | 15pt        | 20pt        | Regular (400)| ✅ Apple HIG · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :219](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L219)) |
| `.headline`    | 13pt        | 16pt        | Bold (700)   | ✅ Apple HIG JSON confirms Bold; emphasized weight is Heavy. WWDC 2020 Session 10175 discusses `.body`+bold→SemiBold, not `.headline` itself. iOS `.headline` is SemiBold, but macOS differs. The OS probe: `.SFNS-Bold`, `NSFontWeightTrait` 0.3 on macOS 14.8.9 and 0.4 on 15.7.9, 26.6.2 and 27.0.1 ([probe 14 :229-231](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L229-L231), [probe 15 :231](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-15/probe.toml#L231)) · macOS 26: same, weight trait 0.4 ([probe 26 :230-231](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L230-L231)) |
| `.subheadline` | 11pt        | 14pt        | Regular (400)| ✅ Apple HIG · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :241](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L241)) |
| `.body`        | 13pt        | 16pt        | Regular (400)| ✅ Apple HIG; WWDC 2020 confirms · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :252](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L252)) |
| `.callout`     | 12pt        | 15pt        | Regular (400)| ✅ Apple HIG · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :263](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L263)) |
| `.footnote`    | 10pt        | 13pt        | Regular (400)| ✅ Apple HIG · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :274](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L274)) |
| `.caption1`    | 10pt        | 13pt        | Regular (400)| ✅ Apple HIG JSON confirms 10pt Regular; emphasized weight is Medium. Third-party impls (ViewKit 9pt, shaps80 8pt) were pre-macOS-11 approximations. · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :285](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L285)) |
| `.caption2`    | 10pt        | 13pt        | Medium (500) | ✅ Apple HIG JSON confirms 10pt Medium (500); emphasized weight is SemiBold. Same size as caption1, differentiated by weight. Third-party impls (ViewKit 8pt, shaps80 7pt) were wrong. · macOS 26: same in the HIG ([HIG typography](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/design/human-interface-guidelines/typography.json), read 2026-10-11); the probe reports `.SFNS-Medium` 10pt with weight trait 0.0 ([probe 26 :293-297](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L293-L297)) |

**Font weight** is obtained from `NSFontDescriptor` traits dictionary. ✅ (macOS 26: same, [docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nsfontdescriptor/traitkey.json), read 2026-10-11)

light/dark appearance):

| `windowBackgroundColor`               | Window background                          | ✅ · macOS 26: same ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nscolor/windowbackgroundcolor.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| `labelColor`                          | Primary text                               | ✅ · macOS 26: same ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nscolor/labelcolor.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| `secondaryLabelColor`                 | Secondary/muted text                       | ✅ · macOS 26: same ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nscolor/secondarylabelcolor.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| `tertiaryLabelColor`                  | Tertiary text                              | ✅ · macOS 26: same ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nscolor/tertiarylabelcolor.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| `quaternaryLabelColor`                | Quaternary text                            | ✅ · macOS 26: same ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nscolor/quaternarylabelcolor.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| `controlColor`                        | Button/control background                  | ✅ catalog color (NSColorType.catalog) — must convert via `colorUsingColorSpace:` before reading RGB · macOS 26: same; `colorUsingColorSpace:` resolves it to #ffffff / #ffffff3f ([probe 26 :334](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L334), :408) |

Colors are resolved per-appearance via
`NSAppearance.performAsCurrentDrawingAppearance`. ✅ (macOS 26: same, [docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nsappearance/performascurrentdrawingappearance(_:).json), read 2026-10-11)
P3 colors are converted to sRGB via `colorUsingColorSpace:`. ✅ (macOS 26: same, [docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nscolor/usingcolorspace(_:).json), read 2026-10-11)

Most system colors date to macOS 10.10+. ✅ Later additions: (macOS 26: same, the NSColor docs)
`systemTealColor` (macOS 10.12); ✅ Apple SDK headers: `API_AVAILABLE(macos(10.12))`; class-dump confirms symbol in AppKit binary pre-10.15; WWDC 2019 Session 210 called it "new" because the header declaration first shipped in the 10.15 SDK, but the runtime symbol existed since 10.12 (macOS 26: same)
`systemMintColor` (macOS 10.12); ✅ same pattern as `systemTealColor` — runtime symbol present in AppKit 1504 (macOS 10.12) per [w0lfschild class-dump](https://github.com/w0lfschild/macOS_headers/blob/master/macOS/Frameworks/AppKit/1504.82.104/NSColor.h); absent from AppKit 1348 (macOS 10.10); SDK header first appeared in macOS 12.0 SDK with `API_AVAILABLE(macos(10.12))`; Apple docs JSON `introducedAt: "10.12"` is correct (not a metadata bug — matches the class-dump evidence) (macOS 26: same)
`systemIndigoColor` (macOS 10.15); ✅ per WWDC 2019 Session 210 and SDK headers `API_AVAILABLE(macos(10.15))` (macOS 26: same)
`systemCyanColor` (macOS 12); ✅ Apple docs JSON correctly shows 12.0; no class-dump evidence of pre-12 existence (unlike teal/mint) (macOS 26: same)

**Text insertion point color:**

| API                                   | What it provides                           |   |
|---------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------|---|
| `NSTextView.insertionPointColor`      | Per-view caret color (instance property) | ✅ very old API; the OS probe: a new NSTextView's is #007aff, `textInsertionPointColor`'s value, on 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :379](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L379), :329; dark :453, :403), not `controlTextColor` · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :379](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L379)) |
| `NSColor.textInsertionPointColor`     | System text insertion point color (type property; macOS 14+) | ✅ Apple docs JSON: `introducedAt: "14.0"`, `roleHeading: "Type Property"` · macOS 26: same ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nscolor/textinsertionpointcolor.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| `NSTextInsertionIndicator`            | System caret view (macOS 14+; follows accent color by default) | ✅ · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value |

macOS 14 changed the caret to match the system accent color and exposed
`NSColor.textInsertionPointColor` as a new type property. macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :329](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L329)).

#### 1.1.3 Geometry

macOS has **no system APIs** for corner radius, border width, or
spacing. These values come from AppKit intrinsic control sizes and
Apple HIG documentation:

| Property              | Source                                  | Value         |   |
|-----------------------|-----------------------------------------|---------------|---|
| Window corner radius  | macOS window manager **(measured)**      | 10px          | ✅ multiple sources confirm through Sequoia; macOS Tahoe (26) uses variable radii per window style — 16pt (title-bar-only) confirmed by [macos-corner-fix](https://github.com/m4rkw/macos-corner-fix); toolbar window radii: sources disagree on exact values — [Zed discussion #38233](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/discussions/38233) reports ~26pt (from WWDC25 Session 310 screenshot), [Podfeet/Steve Harris measurement](https://www.podfeet.com/blog/2025/10/rounded-screenshots-shell-script/) measured 50px at 2× = 25pt, [VS Code PR #270236](https://github.com/microsoft/vscode/pull/270236) suggests 20pt (compact toolbar) and 24pt (standard toolbar); [lapcatsoftware](https://lapcatsoftware.com/articles/2026/3/1.html) describes variable radii qualitatively (toolbar > titlebar-only) without exact values; [alt-tab-macos #4985](https://github.com/lwouis/alt-tab-macos/issues/4985) notes "4 or 5" distinct radii — system may have more tiers than documented here; no public API exists (WebKit reads them dynamically via private `_cornerConfiguration` SPI) · macOS 26: ❓ — the Tahoe figures above are third-party measurements, not a primary source |
| Control corner radius | AppKit intrinsic rendering **(measured)**| 5px           | ❓ no AppKit source: WebKit's `baseBorderRadius = 5` ([`RenderThemeMac.mm:1351`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/de320ba3ff90229f983988d42d4b0f83315459da/Source/WebCore/rendering/mac/RenderThemeMac.mm#L1351), used at :1460) is the radius of a CSS-styled `<select>` only; the OS probe (macOS 14.8.9) reads no radius, and its push-button render's corner, whose edge blends into a soft shadow, gives none to measure ([render](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/renders/aqua/push_button-regular@2x.png)) · macOS 26: 6 **(WebKit)** for the push button and the pop-up, 5 for the text field ([`RenderThemeCocoa.mm:1821`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/de320ba3ff90229f983988d42d4b0f83315459da/Source/WebCore/rendering/cocoa/RenderThemeCocoa.mm#L1821), [:1949](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/de320ba3ff90229f983988d42d4b0f83315459da/Source/WebCore/rendering/cocoa/RenderThemeCocoa.mm#L1949)), WebKit's own vector drawing, compiled for macOS ≥ 26 only; the 26.6.2 probe's push-button and text-field renders are consistent with it ([push button](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/renders/aqua/push_button-regular@2x.png), [text field](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/renders/aqua/text_field-regular@2x.png)) |
| Frame/border width    | AppKit rendering **(measured)**          | 0.5px         | ❓ measured, no Apple docs · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value |
| Scrollbar width       | NSScroller legacy style **(API)**       | 15px          | ✅ `scrollerWidth(for: .regular, scrollerStyle: .legacy)` returns 15 on macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :481](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L481)); a [developer measurement](https://gist.github.com/martynchamberlin/6aaf8a45b36907e9f1e21a28889f6b0a) gave 16, which the API does not · macOS 26: 17 on 26.6.2 and 27.0.1 ([probe 26 :481](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L481)) |
| Scrollbar width       | NSScroller overlay style **(measured)** | ~7px (idle thumb) | ✅ Gecko [`ScrollbarDrawingCocoa.cpp`](https://searchfox.org/mozilla-central/source/widget/ScrollbarDrawingCocoa.cpp): overlay non-hovered thumb = 7px, hovered = 11px; Chromium [`native_theme_mac.mm`](https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/master/ui/native_theme/native_theme_mac.mm): `GetThumbMinSize()` = 6px; two engines agree on ~6–7px; `scrollerWidth(for: .regular, scrollerStyle: .overlay)` returns 16 on macOS 14.8.9, not 0 ([probe 14 :482](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L482)) · macOS 26: `scrollerWidth(for: .regular, scrollerStyle: .overlay)` 17 ([probe 26 :482](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L482)); the idle thumb's width ❓ |
| Focus ring width      | AppKit rendering **(measured)**          | 3px           | ✅ confirmed via WebKit SPI `UIFocusRingStyle.borderThickness = 3`; Mozilla Bug 53927 (Mac OS 9 era, 2px) is obsolete; modern macOS focus ring is a diffuse glow — 3px is the settled border thickness, visual extent is larger · macOS 26: same, WebKit's constant has no version condition ([`RenderTheme.h:207-208`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/de320ba3ff90229f983988d42d4b0f83315459da/Source/WebCore/rendering/RenderTheme.h#L207-L208)) |
| Focus ring offset     | AppKit rendering **(measured)**          | -1px (inset)  | ❓ measured, no Apple docs; WebKit [`RenderThemeCocoa.mm`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/main/Source/WebCore/rendering/cocoa/RenderThemeCocoa.mm) notes "macOS controls have never honored outline offset" — focus ring drawn via `drawFocusRingMask()` with no public offset parameter · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value |
| Disabled opacity      | `disabledControlTextColor` **(API)**     | none (1.0)    | ✅ no API gives a global disabled opacity; AppKit dims by colour: `disabledControlTextColor` is black / white at alpha 63/255 = 0.247 on macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :338, :412](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L338), [probe 15 :338, :412](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-15/probe.toml#L338)), as a [measurement](https://gist.github.com/andrejilderda/8677c565cddc969e6aae7df48622d47c) gave; the "≈0.3 overall" had no source · macOS 26: same, `disabledControlTextColor` #0000003f / #ffffff3f on 26.6.2 and 27.0.1 ([probe 26 :338](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L338), :412) |
| Drop shadows          | compositor-managed                      | yes           | ✅ · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value |

Scrollbar mode depends on user preference (System Preferences →
General → Show scroll bars) and input device (trackpad → overlay,
mouse → legacy).

#### 1.1.4 Widget Metrics

From AppKit intrinsic content sizes (not directly queryable as numbers,
measured from rendered controls):

| Widget           | Property            | Value              |   |
|------------------|---------------------|--------------------|---|
| NSButton         | intrinsic height    | 20px (regular size)| ✅ **(API)** `intrinsicContentSize` 61 × 20, the alignment rect inside a 75 × 32 frame (insets 5 top, 7 right, bottom and left), on macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :1057-1062](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1057-L1062)); the render's bezel is 20 tall, with a soft shadow outside it ([render](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/renders/aqua/push_button-regular@2x.png)); WebKit's `buttonSizes()` regular 20 agrees ([`RenderThemeMac.mm:1009`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/de320ba3ff90229f983988d42d4b0f83315459da/Source/WebCore/rendering/mac/RenderThemeMac.mm#L1009)) · macOS 26: 24 **(API)** `intrinsicContentSize` 65 × 24, the alignment rect the whole frame (insets 0), on 26.6.2 and 27.0.1 ([probe 26 :1057-1062](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L1057-L1062)); WebKit's 20 is its own CSS box |
| NSButton         | horizontal padding  | 8px | ❓ The legacy HIG 12px is inter-button *spacing*, not internal padding. Gecko [`nsNativeThemeCocoa.mm`](https://searchfox.org/mozilla-central/source/widget/cocoa/nsNativeThemeCocoa.mm) `pushButtonSettings` margins `IntMargin{0,5,2,5}` are *external* drawing-rect inflation (for focus rings/chrome), not content padding; Gecko's actual CSS content padding for `<button>` is `padding-inline: 4px` ([`forms.css`](https://searchfox.org/mozilla-central/source/layout/style/res/forms.css)). WebKit [`RenderThemeMac.mm`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/main/Source/WebCore/rendering/mac/RenderThemeMac.mm) `controlPadding(PushButton)` = **8px** horizontal (comment: "Just use 8px. AppKit wants to use 11px for mini buttons, but that padding is just too large for real-world Web sites"); WebKit `cellOutsets` `{5,7,7,7}` are also *external* outsets. Native NSButton bezel internal padding is not directly queryable; best browser-engine evidence points to **~8px** (WebKit) as the closest approximation of the native value · macOS 14–15: 8 **(API)**, `titleRectForBounds:` x 15 against the alignment rect's x 7 ([probe 14 :1062](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1062), :1071) · macOS 26: 12 **(API)** left and right, `titleRectForBounds:` [12, 4, 41, 16] in the 65 × 24 bezel, which is the frame ([probe 26 :1061-1071](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L1061-L1071)); WebKit's vector button pads 6 ([`RenderThemeCocoa.mm:3357-3358`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/de320ba3ff90229f983988d42d4b0f83315459da/Source/WebCore/rendering/cocoa/RenderThemeCocoa.mm#L3357-L3358)), its own HTML button |
| NSTextField      | intrinsic height    | 21px | ✅ **(API)** `intrinsicContentSize` height 21 on macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :1308](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1308)); WebKit's 22 / 19 / 17 are the search field's (22 on 14.8.9, [probe 14 :1344](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1344)) · macOS 26: 24 ([probe 26 :1308](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L1308)) |
| NSTextField      | horizontal padding  | 2px | ✅ **(API)** `NSTextFieldCell titleRectForBounds:` [2, 2, 156, 17] in the 160 × 21 field on macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :1323](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1323)) · macOS 26: 4 ([4, 4, 152, 16] in 160 × 24, [probe 26 :1323](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L1323)); WebKit's vector text field pads 0.5em, its own |
| NSButton (switch)| checkbox indicator  | 14px               | ✅ **(API)** `imageRectForBounds:` 14 × 14 on macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :1107](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1107)), as WebKit's regular 14; Gecko's 16 is not AppKit's · macOS 26: 16 ([probe 26 :1107](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L1107)) |
| NSButton (switch)| label spacing       | 6px | ✅ **(API)** the title rect starts at x 22, 6 after the 14-wide image rect's right edge at 16, on macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :1106-1107](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1106-L1107)) · macOS 26: 6 (image rect 0-16, title from 22, [probe 26 :1106-1107](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L1106-L1107); the render's first glyph column is x 22, [render](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/renders/aqua/checkbox-regular-off@2x.png)) |
| NSSlider         | track height        | 4px | ✅ **(API)** `barRectFlipped:` 4 high on macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :1435](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1435)); WebKit's `sliderTrackWidth = 5` is its own track · macOS 26: 6 ([probe 26 :1435](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L1435); the render's track is 12 px at 2×, rows 26-37, [render](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/renders/aqua/slider-regular@2x.png)); WebKit's vector track is 8 |
| NSSlider         | thumb diameter      | 20px               | ✅ **(measured)** the OS probe's render, macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9: the knob is a disk 40 px across at 2× in both axes, a soft shadow outside it ([render](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/renders/aqua/slider-regular@2x.png)); `knobThickness` returns 24, the knob's 24 × 28 rect ([probe 14 :1423](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1423), :1436), and WebKit's `sliderThumbThickness = 17` has a FIXME that it should come from AppKit ([`RenderThemeMac.mm:1650-1652`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/de320ba3ff90229f983988d42d4b0f83315459da/Source/WebCore/rendering/mac/RenderThemeMac.mm#L1650-L1652)) · macOS 26: a capsule 20 wide × 16 high, not a disk: `knobRectFlipped:` 20 × 16, `knobThickness` 20 ([probe 26 :1423](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L1423), :1436), the render's knob 40 × 32 px at 2× ([render](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/renders/aqua/slider-regular@2x.png)); WebKit draws 24 × 16 |
| NSSlider         | tick mark length    | 8px                | ✅ **(measured)** the OS probe's render, macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9: each tick is drawn 2 wide and 8 long, centred in the 2 × 16 rect `rectOfTickMarkAtIndex:` returns ([probe 14 :1481](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1481), [render](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/renders/aqua/slider_ticks-regular@2x.png)); WebKit's 1 × 3 (`sliderTickSize`) is its own `<datalist>` tick · macOS 26: 2 — `rectOfTickMarkAtIndex:` is 2 × 2 ([probe 26 :1481](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L1481)) and the render draws each tick as a 2-pt dot below the track (rows 44-47 at 2×, [render](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/renders/aqua/slider_ticks-regular@2x.png)) |
| NSProgressIndicator | bar height       | 6px (visual track) | ✅ **(measured)** the OS probe's render, macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9: the bar is 12 px at 2× (rows 30-41 on 14, 28-39 on 15) inside a 20-high frame (18 on 15) ([render](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/renders/aqua/progress_bar-regular@2x.png); [probe 14 :1503](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1503)) · macOS 26: 8 (the render's bar is 16 px at 2×, rows 28-43, in a 20-high frame, [render](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/renders/aqua/progress_bar-regular@2x.png), [probe 26 :1501](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L1501)), as WebKit's vector bar ([`RenderThemeCocoa.mm:3770-3773`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/de320ba3ff90229f983988d42d4b0f83315459da/Source/WebCore/rendering/cocoa/RenderThemeCocoa.mm#L3770-L3773)) |
| NSScroller       | track width (legacy)| 15px               | ✅ **(API)** `scrollerWidth(for: .regular, scrollerStyle: .legacy)` returns 15 on macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :481](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L481)); a [developer measurement](https://gist.github.com/martynchamberlin/6aaf8a45b36907e9f1e21a28889f6b0a) gave 16 · macOS 26: 17 ([probe 26 :481](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L481)) |

| NSProgressIndicator | spinning small   | 16px diameter       | ✅ `sizeToFit` with `NSControlSizeSmall` · the OS probe: 16 on 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :1028](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1028)) · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :1028](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L1028)) |
| NSPopUpButton    | intrinsic height    | 20px (regular size) | ✅ **(API)** `intrinsicContentSize` 73 × 20, the alignment rect, on macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :1237](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1237), :1242; the render's fill is rows 18-57 at 2×, [render](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/renders/aqua/pop_up_button-regular@2x.png)); WebKit's regular 21 is its own · macOS 26: 24 ([probe 26 :1237](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L1237)) |
| NSPopUpButton    | arrow area width    | ~16–18px            | ❓ measured visible indicator; note: WebKit total right-padding is 26px (includes arrow + surrounding space); the OS probe's render (macOS 14.8.9) shows no boundary of an arrow area to measure ([render](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/renders/aqua/pop_up_button-regular@2x.png)) · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value |
| NSSegmentedControl | segment height    | 22px (regular size) | ✅ **(API)** `intrinsicContentSize` 75 × 22, the alignment rect, in a 24-high frame, on macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 ([probe 14 :1380](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1380), :1385) · macOS 26: 24, the alignment rect the frame ([probe 26 :1380](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L1380), :1385); 27 draws it wider ([probe 27 :1380](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-xcode-27/probe.toml#L1380)) |
| NSSegmentedControl | separator width   | 1px                 | ❓ measured · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value |
| NSDisclosureButton | triangle size     | ~13px               | ❓ measured visible triangle; Gecko `nsNativeThemeCocoa.mm`: cell=21×21px — visible triangle is a subset of the cell · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value |

#### 1.1.5 Layout Spacing

Apple HIG defines specific spacing values per context (not a system
API — design documentation only):

| Context                          | Recommended spacing |   |
|----------------------------------|---------------------|---|
| Between related controls         | 8pt                 | ❓ oversimplified: HIG says 12px for regular push buttons, 8px for mini/icon · macOS 26: same, `NSStackView.spacing`'s default 8 ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nsstackview/spacing.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| Between unrelated groups         | 20pt                | ❓ 20pt is documented as superview-edge margin, not specifically inter-group · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value |
| Label to its associated control  | 8pt                 | ✅ HIG: 8px for regular, 6px small, 5px mini · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value |
| Content margin (window edge)     | 20pt                | ✅ Auto Layout Guide confirms · macOS 26: same (the Auto Layout Guide, unversioned) |
| Compact spacing (toolbar items)  | 8pt                 | ✅ HIG: "8 pixels between toolbar controls" · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value |

Interface Builder's "standard spacing" constraint corresponds to 8pt. ✅ Auto Layout Guide confirms (macOS 26: same, unversioned)
NSStackView default spacing is 8pt. ✅ Apple docs: "default value is 8.0 points" (macOS 26: same, [docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nsstackview/spacing.json), read 2026-10-11)

#### 1.1.6 Icon Sizes

macOS has no per-context icon size constants. Sizes come from
container conventions and SF Symbols automatic sizing:

| Context                 | Source                        | Size    |   |
|-------------------------|-------------------------------|---------|---|
| Toolbar (regular mode)  | `NSToolbar` convention        | 32pt    | ✅ NSToolbar.SizeMode docs (deprecated) · macOS 26: same; deprecated in macOS 27 ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nstoolbar/sizemode-swift.enum/regular.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| Toolbar (small mode)    | `NSToolbar` convention        | 24pt    | ✅ NSToolbar.SizeMode docs (deprecated) · macOS 26: same; deprecated in macOS 27 ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nstoolbar/sizemode-swift.enum/small.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| Sidebar (small)         | Apple HIG sidebar metrics     | 16×16px (row: 24pt) | ✅ Apple HIG Sidebars page (macOS, 2022 archived); metrics table removed from current HIG ~2024 · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value: the current HIG gives no sidebar metrics ([HIG sidebars](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/design/human-interface-guidelines/sidebars.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| Sidebar (medium)        | Apple HIG sidebar metrics     | 20×20px (row: 28pt) | ✅ same source; pre-Big Sur was 18pt (legacy CoreTypes.bundle sizes) · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value (as the small row) |
| Sidebar (large)         | Apple HIG sidebar metrics     | 24×24px (row: 32pt) | ✅ same source; pre-Big Sur was 32pt · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value (as the small row) |
| Menu item               | SF Symbols in menus           | ~13pt   | ❓ inferred from system font size · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value; on 27 NSMenu hides item images by default |
| Menu bar extra          | Status item convention        | 16pt    | ❓ community best-practice (Bjango), not official · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value |

SF Symbols sizes are automatic when placed in native containers
(`NSToolbarItem`, sidebar). Manual sizing via
`NSImage.SymbolConfiguration(pointSize:weight:scale:)`.

#### 1.1.7 Accessibility

| Setting                            | API                                                          |   |
|------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------|---|
| Text styles                        | `NSFont.preferredFont(forTextStyle:)` returns role-based fonts | ✅ macOS 11+; sizes are fixed — macOS does not support Dynamic Type (WWDC 2020 confirms) · macOS 26: same ([HIG typography](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/design/human-interface-guidelines/typography.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| Reduce motion                      | `NSWorkspace.accessibilityDisplayShouldReduceMotion`         | ✅ macOS 10.12 · macOS 26: same ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace/accessibilitydisplayshouldreducemotion.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| Reduce transparency                | `NSWorkspace.accessibilityDisplayShouldReduceTransparency`   | ✅ macOS 10.10 · macOS 26: same ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace/accessibilitydisplayshouldreducetransparency.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| Increase contrast                  | `NSWorkspace.accessibilityDisplayShouldIncreaseContrast`     | ✅ macOS 10.10 · macOS 26: same ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace/accessibilitydisplayshouldincreasecontrast.json), read 2026-10-11) |
| Differentiate without color        | `NSWorkspace.accessibilityDisplayShouldDifferentiateWithoutColor` | ✅ macOS 10.10 · macOS 26: same ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace/accessibilitydisplayshoulddifferentiatewithoutcolor.json), read 2026-10-11) |

---

### 1.2 Windows

#### 1.2.1 Fonts

**NONCLIENTMETRICSW** (from `SystemParametersInfoW(SPI_GETNONCLIENTMETRICS)`): ✅

Five separate LOGFONTW entries, each with `lfFaceName` (family),
`lfHeight` (size in logical units), `lfWeight` (weight 0–1000): ✅

| Field            | Role              | Typical default             |   |
|------------------|-------------------|-----------------------------|---|
| `lfMessageFont`  | Body/dialog text  | ⚙ Segoe UI, lfHeight=-12, 400 | ✅ **(API)** Segoe UI, -12, 400 at 96 DPI on the three Windows probe images, `windows-2022` (Server 2022, build 20348), `windows-2025` (Server 2025, 26100) and `windows-11-arm` (Windows 11 25H2, 26200) ([probe.toml:533-542](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L533-L542), the same line in each image's file); the [Win32 UX Guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/uxguide/vis-fonts), written for Vista and 7, gives "9 pt. Segoe UI" |
| `lfCaptionFont`  | Window title bar  | ⚙ Segoe UI, lfHeight=-12, 400 | ✅ **(API)** Segoe UI, -12, 400 on the three Windows probe images, Windows 11 25H2 included ([probe.toml:489-498](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L489-L498)); ❌ the earlier "700 (Bold) on Windows 11", from a [Microsoft Q&A](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/answers/questions/5489781/title-bar-text-boldness-in-windows-11) forum answer about how the title bar looks, is not what the API returns |
| `lfSmCaptionFont`| Tool window title | ⚙ Segoe UI, lfHeight=-12, 400 | ✅ **(API)** on the three Windows probe images ([probe.toml:500-509](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L500-L509)) |
| `lfMenuFont`     | Menu items        | ⚙ Segoe UI, lfHeight=-12, 400 | ✅ **(API)** on the three Windows probe images ([probe.toml:511-520](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L511-L520)) |
| `lfStatusFont`   | Status bar and tooltip text | ⚙ Segoe UI, lfHeight=-12, 400 | ✅ **(API)** on the three Windows probe images ([probe.toml:522-531](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L522-L531)); NONCLIENTMETRICSW: "the font used in status bars and tooltips" |

Note: Win11 uses Segoe UI Variable internally in WinUI3/XAML controls,
but the Win32 `SystemParametersInfoW` API returns "Segoe UI": all five fonts on Windows 11 25H2 ([probe.toml:489-542](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L489-L542), `windows-11-arm`).

Size conversion: `points = abs(lfHeight) * 72 / dpi` ↕. ✅ derived from LOGFONTW docs
At 96 DPI: abs(-12) * 72 / 96 = 9pt. ✅ native-theme's Windows reader reads these fonts at 96 DPI (`SystemParametersInfoForDpi(SPI_GETNONCLIENTMETRICS, …, 96)`) and reports a `font_dpi` of 96 (`LOGICAL_DPI`, `native-theme/src/windows.rs`), so its sizes are logical pixels (a point is 96/72 epx) at any display scale, not device pixels at the system DPI.

**WinUI3 Fluent Design type ramp** (design guidelines, not system API):

| Ramp name  | Size  | Weight       | Line height |   |
|------------|-------|--------------|-------------|---|
| Caption    | 12px  | Regular (400)| 16px        | ✅ |
| Body       | 14px  | Regular (400)| 20px        | ✅ |
| BodyStrong | 14px  | SemiBold(600)| 20px        | ✅ |
| BodyLarge  | 18px  | Regular (400)| 24px        | ✅ |
| BodyLargeStrong | 18px | SemiBold(600)| 24px   | ✅ |
| Subtitle   | 20px  | SemiBold(600)| 28px        | ✅ |
| Title      | 28px  | SemiBold(600)| 36px        | ✅ |
| TitleLarge | 40px  | SemiBold(600)| 52px        | ✅ |
| Display    | 68px  | SemiBold(600)| 92px        | ✅ |

All confirmed via MS Typography docs. All sizes are in effective pixels
(epx), which equal physical pixels at 100% scaling (96 DPI). The model's sizes are logical pixels, which are epx; the Windows reader's `font_dpi` of 96 (above) keeps a converted point size in that unit.

There is **no system monospace font setting** on Windows. ✅ Applications
choose their own (typically Consolas or Cascadia Mono).

#### 1.2.2 Colors

**UISettings (WinRT):** ✅

| Method / Value        | What it provides               |   |
|-----------------------|--------------------------------|---|
| `GetColorValue(Foreground)` | System foreground color  | ✅ |
| `GetColorValue(Background)` | System background color  | ✅ |
| `GetColorValue(Accent)`     | Accent color             | ✅ |
| `GetColorValue(AccentDark1/2/3)` | Darker accent shades | ✅ |
| `GetColorValue(AccentLight1/2/3)`| Lighter accent shades| ✅ |
| `GetColorValue(Complement)`     | Complement (not supported, do not use) | ✅ exists but docs say "Do not use" |

**GetSysColor (Win32):** ✅

| Constant             | What it provides                    |   |
|----------------------|-------------------------------------|---|
| `COLOR_WINDOW`       | Text input / view background        | ✅ |
| `COLOR_WINDOWTEXT`   | Text input / view foreground        | ✅ |
| `COLOR_BTNFACE`      | Button background                   | ✅ |
| `COLOR_BTNTEXT`      | Button foreground                   | ✅ |
| `COLOR_HIGHLIGHT`    | Selection background                | ✅ |
| `COLOR_HIGHLIGHTTEXT`| Selection foreground                | ✅ |
| `COLOR_GRAYTEXT`     | Disabled text                       | ✅ |
| `COLOR_MENU`         | Menu background                     | ✅ |
| `COLOR_MENUTEXT`     | Menu foreground                     | ✅ |
| `COLOR_SCROLLBAR`    | Scrollbar track                     | ✅ |
| `COLOR_INFOBK`       | Tooltip background                  | ✅ |
| `COLOR_INFOTEXT`     | Tooltip foreground                  | ✅ |
| `COLOR_ACTIVECAPTION`| Active title bar background         | ✅ |
| `COLOR_CAPTIONTEXT`  | Active title bar text               | ✅ |
| `COLOR_INACTIVECAPTION`| Inactive title bar background     | ✅ |
| `COLOR_INACTIVECAPTIONTEXT`| Inactive title bar text        | ✅ |
| `COLOR_3DSHADOW`     | 3D shadow edge                      | ✅ |
| `COLOR_3DHIGHLIGHT`  | 3D highlight edge                   | ✅ |
| `COLOR_HOTLIGHT`     | Hot-tracked / hyperlink color       | ✅ |

On Windows 10+, only `COLOR_WINDOW`, `COLOR_WINDOWTEXT`, `COLOR_HIGHLIGHT`,
`COLOR_HIGHLIGHTTEXT`, `COLOR_3DFACE`, `COLOR_GRAYTEXT`, `COLOR_BTNTEXT`,
and `COLOR_HOTLIGHT` are supported. ✅ MSDN confirms (8 constants). Note:
`COLOR_3DFACE` (value 15) is not marked "not supported", but its alias
`COLOR_BTNFACE` (same value 15) is — a documentation inconsistency.
The remaining constants listed above (`COLOR_MENU`,
`COLOR_MENUTEXT`, `COLOR_SCROLLBAR`, `COLOR_INFOBK`, `COLOR_INFOTEXT`,
`COLOR_ACTIVECAPTION`, `COLOR_CAPTIONTEXT`, `COLOR_INACTIVECAPTION`,
`COLOR_INACTIVECAPTIONTEXT`, `COLOR_3DSHADOW`, `COLOR_3DHIGHLIGHT`) are
annotated "not supported" by MSDN — they still return values but may not
reflect the actual system appearance.

**DWM:**

| Function                  | What it provides                   |   |
|---------------------------|------------------------------------|---|
| `DwmGetColorizationColor` | Glass composition colour; the reader does not read it (§2.2 `title_bar_background`) | ❓ no source ties it to the title bar; #006fc4, alpha 0xe3, on `windows-11-arm`, where `ColorPrevalence` (accent on title bars) is 0 ([probe.toml:763](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L763), [probe.toml:773](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L773)) |

**GetSystemMetricsForDpi (Win32):**

| Metric           | What it provides              | Typical at 96 DPI |   |
|------------------|-------------------------------|--------------------|---|
| `SM_CXBORDER`    | Border width                  | ↕ 1px              | ✅ **(API)** 1 at 96 DPI on the three Windows probe images ([probe.toml:24-29](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L24-L29)); NONCLIENTMETRICSW's `iBorderWidth` is the sizing border, not this metric |

| `SM_CXVSCROLL`   | Vertical scrollbar width      | ↕ 17px             | ✅ **(API)** 17 at 96 DPI on the three Windows probe images ([probe.toml:101-106](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L101-L106)) |

| `SM_CYMENU`      | Menu bar height               | ↕ 20px             | ✅ **(API)** 20 at 96 DPI on the three Windows probe images ([probe.toml:185-190](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L185-L190)); `iMenuHeight` 19 ([probe.toml:486](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L486)). MSDN: "height of a single-line menu bar… not the height of a menu item" — Win32 dropdown menu items derive height from font + padding, not this metric |
| `SM_CXFOCUSBORDER`| Focus border width           | ↕ 1px              | ✅ **(API)** 1 at 96 DPI on the three Windows probe images ([probe.toml:52-57](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L52-L57)) |
| `SM_CYFOCUSBORDER`| Focus border height          | ↕ 1px              | ✅ **(API)** on the three Windows probe images ([probe.toml:59-64](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L59-L64)) |
| `SM_CXSMICON`    | Small icon width              | ↕ 16px             | ✅ standard Windows icon size |
| `SM_CYSMICON`    | Small icon height             | ↕ 16px             | ✅ same |

**GetDpiForSystem:** Returns system DPI (96 = 100% scaling). ✅

**Hardcoded WinUI3 Fluent values** (not queryable, design guidelines):

| Property          | Value |   |
|-------------------|-------|---|
| Control radius    | 4px   | ✅ MS Geometry docs: ControlCornerRadius=4px |
| Overlay radius    | 8px   | ✅ MS Geometry docs: OverlayCornerRadius=8px |
| Shadow            | yes   | ✅ |
| Disabled opacity  | per-control; `ListViewItemDisabledThemeOpacity`=0.3 | ❓ no global disabled opacity; WinUI3 uses per-control `*Disabled` color brushes; 0.55 was legacy Win8.x/UWP — current WinUI3 value is 0.3 |

WinUI3 focus visual primary border is 2px ✅ (differs from Win32
`SM_CXFOCUSBORDER` = 1px). Secondary border is 1px inside. ✅

#### 1.2.4 Widget Metrics

**From system metrics + WinUI3 Fluent defaults:**

| Widget          | Property            | Source                        | Value    |   |
|-----------------|---------------------|-------------------------------|----------|---|
| Button          | min height          | WinUI3 default                | ~27px (effective) | ❓ no `ButtonMinHeight` resource; effective=14px text + 5+6 padding + 2 border = 27px; `ContentDialogButtonHeight=32` is dialog-specific |
| Button          | horizontal padding  | WinUI3 default                | 11px     | ✅ ButtonPadding=11,5,11,6 |
| Button          | vertical padding    | WinUI3 default                | 5px top, 6px bottom | ✅ same |
| Button          | icon spacing        | WinUI3 default                | 8px      | ❓ hardcoded `Margin="8,0,0,0"` in DropDownButton template; no named XAML resource; consistent with Fluent 2 `size80` spacing |
| CheckBox        | indicator size      | WinUI3 default                | 20px     | ✅ CheckBoxSize=20 |
| CheckBox        | label spacing       | WinUI3 default                | 8px      | ✅ CheckBoxPadding first value=8 |
| TextBox         | min height          | WinUI3 default                | 32px     | ✅ `TextControlThemeMinHeight=32` in generic.xaml |
| TextBox         | horizontal padding  | WinUI3 default                | 10px left, 6px right | ✅ TextControlThemePadding=10,5,6,6 (asymmetric: right is smaller due to delete button column) |
| TextBox         | vertical padding    | WinUI3 default                | 5px top, 6px bottom | ✅ TextControlThemePadding=10,5,6,6 |
| Scrollbar (Win32) | width             | `SM_CXVSCROLL` (DPI-aware)    | ↕ 17px   | ✅ see §1.2.3 |
| Scrollbar (Win32) | thumb height     | `SM_CYVTHUMB` (DPI-aware)     | ↕ 17px   | ✅ see §1.2.3 |
| Scrollbar (WinUI3)| collapsed width  | ScrollBar template (inline)   | ~2px     | ❓ XAML template inline value; expands on pointer proximity |
| Scrollbar (WinUI3)| expanded width   | ScrollBar template (inline)   | ~6px     | ❓ 6 = `ScrollBarSize` 12 less two 3px halves of `ScrollBarThumbStrokeThickness` 6, a derivation no source states; the template's named resources are `ScrollBarSize` 12, `ScrollBarVerticalThumbMinWidth` 8 and `ScrollBarThumbStrokeThickness` 6 ([ScrollBar_themeresources.xaml:180, :182, :185](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/ScrollBar_themeresources.xaml#L180-L185)) |
| Slider          | track height        | WinUI3 default                | 4px      | ✅ SliderTrackThemeHeight=4 |
| Slider          | thumb size          | WinUI3 default                | 18px     | ✅ SliderHorizontalThumbWidth/Height=18 |
| Slider          | tick length         | WinUI3 default                | 4px      | ✅ `SliderOutsideTickBarThemeHeight=4` |
| ProgressBar     | min height (control)| WinUI3 default                | 3px      | ✅ ProgressBarMinHeight=3 |
| ProgressBar     | track height        | WinUI3 default                | 1px      | ✅ ProgressBarTrackHeight=1 |
| TabView         | min height          | WinUI3 default                | 32px     | ✅ TabViewItemMinHeight=32 |
| TabView         | horizontal padding  | WinUI3 default                | 8px left, 4px right | ✅ `TabViewItemHeaderPadding=8,3,4,3` (8/8 without close button) |
| TabView         | vertical padding    | WinUI3 default                | 3px      | ✅ same source |
| Menu item       | height              | Win32: font-derived; WinUI3: padding-derived | ↕ ~20px (Win32) | ❓ Win32 formula (from [Wine `menu.c`](https://github.com/wine-mirror/wine/blob/master/dlls/user32/menu.c) / [ReactOS `menu.c`](https://github.com/nicknisi/reactos/blob/master/win32ss/user/ntuser/menu.c)): `max(text_height + 2, char_height + 4)` → at 96 DPI with Segoe UI 9pt (cell ~16px): max(18, 20) = 20px. `SM_CYMENU` (20px) is menu *bar* height, not item height — the match is coincidental. WinUI3: touch ~31px (`MenuFlyoutItemThemePadding=11,8,11,9` + 14px text), narrow ~23px (`MenuFlyoutItemThemePaddingNarrow=11,4,11,5`) |
| Menu item       | horizontal padding  | WinUI3 default                | 11px     | ✅ `MenuFlyoutItemThemePadding=11,8,11,9` (+ 4px outer `MenuFlyoutItemMargin`) |
| Menu item       | icon spacing        | WinUI3 default                | 12px     | ✅ icon placeholder=28px minus 16px icon = 12px gap |
| ToolTip         | padding             | WinUI3 default                | 9px horiz, 6/8px vert | ✅ ToolTipBorderPadding=9,6,9,8 |
| ToolTip         | max width           | WinUI3 default                | 320px    | ✅ MaxWidth=320 |
| ListView        | item height         | WinUI3 default                | 40px     | ✅ ListViewItemMinHeight=40 |
| ListView        | horizontal padding  | WinUI3 default                | 16px left, 12px right | ✅ the implicit style's `DefaultListViewItemStyle` sets `Padding="16,0,12,0"` ([ListViewItem_themeresources.xaml:233-241](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/ListViewItem_themeresources.xaml#L233-L241)); `12,0,12,0` (:270) belongs to `ListViewItemExpanded`, which no control uses |
| ListView        | vertical padding    | WinUI3 default                | 0px      | ✅ vertical space from `MinHeight=40`, not padding |
| CommandBar      | height (default)    | WinUI3 default                | 48px     | ✅ `DefaultCommandBarStyle` sets `ClosedDisplayMode="Compact"` ([CommandBar_themeresources.xaml:86](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/CommandBar_themeresources.xaml#L86)) and its `ContentRoot` has `MinHeight=AppBarThemeCompactHeight` (:795) = 48 (:72); `AppBarThemeMinHeight=64` (:71) is referenced nowhere else in that file. Microsoft Learn [Command bar](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/controls/command-bar): "**Compact**: The default mode." |
| CommandBar      | height (compact)    | WinUI3 default                | 48px     | ✅ AppBarThemeCompactHeight=48 |
| CommandBar      | item spacing        | WinUI3 default                | 0px      | ✅ StackPanel has no Spacing; visual separation from AppBarButton inner margins (2,6,2,6) |

#### 1.2.5 Layout Spacing

**WinUI3 Fluent spacing tokens** (design guidelines, not system API;
Fluent 2 uses numeric names `sizeNone`..`size320` for the code-implemented subset; the full design ramp extends to `size560`): ✅

| Token name | Value |   |
|------------|-------|---|
| None       | 0px   | ✅ |
| XXSmall    | 2px   | ✅ |
| XSmall     | 4px   | ✅ |
| sNudge     | 6px   | ✅ |
| Small      | 8px   | ✅ |
| mNudge     | 10px  | ✅ |
| Medium     | 12px  | ✅ |
| Large      | 16px  | ✅ |
| XLarge     | 20px  | ✅ |
| XXLarge    | 24px  | ✅ |
| XXXLarge   | 32px  | ✅ |

All pixel values confirmed via FluentUI spacings.ts. Token names are informal shorthand from the internal code keys (`xxs`, `xs`, `s`, etc.) — Fluent 2 design system uses `sizeNone`..`size320` (code subset) or up to `size560` (full ramp, 17 tokens); code exports use `spacingHorizontalXXS` etc.

**What these tokens are for**: This is a value palette for WinUI3
control template authors — a menu of recommended spacing values to
pick from when defining padding, margins, and gaps inside XAML
templates. Individual controls pick specific values from this ramp
(and often use off-ramp values like 11px, 9px, 3px that don't land
on any token). The tokens are not a system API, not user-configurable,
and not exposed at runtime.

**Why we don't implement this ramp**: Every spacing value that matters
is already captured as a direct per-widget field — `button.padding_horizontal`
= 11px (from `ButtonPadding`), `dialog.button_spacing` = 8px (from
`ContentDialogButtonSpacing`), `menu.icon_spacing` = 12px, etc. (see
§2.3–2.28). The abstract ramp adds no information beyond what the
per-widget fields already provide. Windows has no layout container
defaults either — `StackPanel.Spacing` defaults to 0 — so unlike KDE
(§1.3.5) there are no global layout constants to capture in §2.20.

#### 1.2.6 Icon Sizes

**GetSystemMetrics (Win32):**

| Metric           | What it provides    | Typical at 96 DPI |   |
|------------------|---------------------|--------------------|---|
| `SM_CXICON`      | Large icon width    | ↕ 32px             | ✅ |
| `SM_CYICON`      | Large icon height   | ↕ 32px             | ✅ |
| `SM_CXSMICON`    | Small icon width    | ↕ 16px             | ✅ |
| `SM_CYSMICON`    | Small icon height   | ↕ 16px             | ✅ |

**Shell image lists** (`SHGetImageList`):

| Constant          | Default size |   |
|-------------------|-------------|---|
| `SHIL_SMALL`      | 16px        | ✅ |
| `SHIL_LARGE`      | 32px        | ✅ |
| `SHIL_EXTRALARGE` | 48px        | ✅ |
| `SHIL_JUMBO`      | 256px       | ✅ |

**WinUI3 Fluent icon contexts** (Segoe Fluent Icons):

| Context              | Typical size |   |
|----------------------|-------------|---|
| `AppBarButton` icon  | 16px        | ✅ `AppBarButtonContentHeight`=16 ([generic.xaml:19462](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/dxaml/xcp/dxaml/themes/generic.xaml#L19462)), the default style's icon `Viewbox` height ([AppBarButton_themeresources.xaml:366](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/AppBarButton_themeresources.xaml#L366)); the [Command bar](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/controls/command-bar) docs' 20×20 is not what the template draws |
| `NavigationViewItem` | 16px        | ✅ `NavigationViewItemOnLeftIconBoxHeight=16` |

#### 1.2.7 Accessibility

| Setting              | API                                        |   |
|----------------------|--------------------------------------------|---|
| Display scale factor | `GetDpiForSystem()` / 96 (96 = 100%)       | ✅ |
| Text scale factor    | `UISettings.TextScaleFactor` (WinRT, 1.0–2.25) | ✅ MSDN confirms range 1.0–2.25 |
| High contrast mode   | `SystemParametersInfoW(SPI_GETHIGHCONTRAST)`| ✅ |
| Reduce motion        | `SystemParametersInfoW(SPI_GETCLIENTAREAANIMATION)` | ✅ |

Display scaling (DPI) affects all UI uniformly. Text scaling is an
independent accessibility setting (Settings → Accessibility → Text size)
that only enlarges text.

### 1.3 KDE

#### 1.3.1 Fonts

**kdeglobals `[General]` section** — `QFont::toString()` format: ✅
`family,pointSizeF,pixelSize,styleHint,weight,style,underline,strikeOut,fixedPitch,...`

Field 0 = family, field 1 = point size, field 4 = weight. ✅ Field 2 = pixel size: `toString` writes `pointSizeF()` then `pixelSize()` ([qfont.cpp:2159-2160](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.cpp#L2159-L2160)), each -1 when the size was set the other way ([:1041-1050](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.cpp#L1041-L1050), [:1080-1090](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.cpp#L1080-L1090)); `fromString` sets the point size when field 1 is positive, then the pixel size when field 2 is positive, which replaces it ([:2252-2257](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.cpp#L2252-L2257); `setPixelSize` sets the point size to -1, [:1074-1075](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.cpp#L1074-L1075)). A pixel size is in pixels, at most an unsigned 16-bit integer ([:1052-1054](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.cpp#L1052-L1054)), not points, so no point-to-pixel DPI conversion applies to it. `fromString` reads fields 2 to 5 only from a string of at least 10 fields: it rejects 3 to 9 fields ([:2245](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.cpp#L2245)) and reads them under `count >= 10` ([:2255](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.cpp#L2255)). Field 5 = style, `QFont::Style`: `StyleNormal` 0, `StyleItalic` 1, `StyleOblique` 2 ([qfont.h:77-81](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.h#L77-L81)), written as `(int) style()` ([qfont.cpp:2163](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.cpp#L2163)) and read back by `setStyle` ([:2263](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.cpp#L2263)). ✅ Qt 6.11.2 (`ef55f427`)
Qt6 adds extra fixed fields (capitalization, letterSpacingType, letterSpacing,
wordSpacing, stretch, styleStrategy, font style, font features, variable axes)
after field 9. Qt6 6.4–6.10 produces 16 fixed fields + an optional
styleName (17th). Qt6 6.11+ (released 2026-03-23) always emits
styleName and adds features/variableAxes counts, producing a minimum of 19
fields. Parser should handle variable field counts gracefully.

Weight scale differs between Qt versions:
- **Qt5**: 0–99 (Normal=50, DemiBold=63, Bold=75, Black=87) ✅ Black (87) is the highest named constant; max accepted value is 99; Qt converts it to the Qt6 scale with `qt_legacyToOpenTypeWeight` when the string has at least 10 fields ([qfont.cpp:2245](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.cpp#L2245), [:2255](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.cpp#L2255)) and fewer than 16 ([qfont.cpp:2259-2262](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.cpp#L2259-L2262)): the nearest of the Qt5 constants 0, 12, 25, 50, 57, 63, 75, 81, 87 gives 100, 200, 300, 400, 500, 600, 700, 800, 900, the lower constant on a tie (`legacyToOpenTypeMap`, `convertWeights`, [:147-174](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.cpp#L147-L174), [:196-200](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.11.2/src/gui/text/qfont.cpp#L196-L200)), so Qt5 Black 87 is Qt6 Black 900 ✅ Qt 6.11.2
- **Qt6**: 1–1000 (Normal=400, DemiBold=600, Bold=700, Black=900) ✅ named constants span 100–900 but range accepts 1–1000

Parser must detect which scale is in use and normalize to CSS 100–900.

| Key                    | Role              | Typical Breeze default                      |   |
|------------------------|-------------------|---------------------------------------------|---|
| `font`                 | Body text         | ⚙ Noto Sans, 10pt, 400                     | ✅ kfontsettingsdata.cpp |
| `fixed`                | Monospace         | ⚙ Hack, 10pt, 400                          | ✅ kfontsettingsdata.cpp |
| `smallestReadableFont` | Smallest text     | ⚙ Noto Sans, 8pt, 400                      | ✅ kfontsettingsdata.cpp |
| `toolBarFont`          | Toolbar labels    | ⚙ Noto Sans, 10pt, 400 (can be smaller)    | ✅ |
| `menuFont`             | Menu items        | ⚙ Noto Sans, 10pt, 400                     | ✅ |
| `taskbarFont`          | Taskbar/panel     | ⚙ Noto Sans, 10pt, 400                     | ✅ in kfontsettingsdata.cpp (not in kcfg GUI) |

**`[WM]` section font:**

| Key                    | Role              | Typical Breeze default                      |   |
|------------------------|-------------------|---------------------------------------------|---|
| `activeFont`           | Window title bar  | ⚙ Noto Sans, 10pt, 400 (Normal)            | ✅ |

#### 1.3.3 Geometry

KDE has **no geometry settings in kdeglobals**. All geometry values
come from the Breeze style engine source code:

| Control corner radius | `Frame_FrameRadius`             | 5px   | ✅ breezemetrics.h |
| Frame/border width    | `PenWidth::Frame`               | 1.001px | ✅ breezemetrics.h |
| Scrollbar groove width| `ScrollBar_Extend`              | 21px  | ✅ breezemetrics.h |
| Focus ring margin     | `PM_FocusFrameHMargin`          | 2px   | ✅ breezemetrics.h |
| Disabled state        | `ColorEffects:Disabled` palette blending | (no single opacity) | ✅ |
| Drop shadows          | yes (KWin compositor)           |       | ✅ |

#### 1.3.5 Layout Spacing

From Breeze source code:

| Constant name              | What it provides              | Value |   |
|----------------------------|-------------------------------|-------|---|

There is **no abstract spacing scale** in KDE. These are specific
layout constants. ✅

#### 1.3.6 Icon Sizes

The active icon theme name is read from `kdeglobals [Icons] Theme`
(default: `breeze`). ✅

**`KIconLoader` groups** — sizes come from the icon theme's own
`index.theme` (`DesktopDefault`, `ToolbarDefault`, etc. in `[Icon Theme]`
section), **not** from `kdeglobals`. C++ fallbacks in `kicontheme.cpp`
are used only when the icon theme omits a key:

| Group / icon theme key     | C++ fallback | Breeze default |   |
|----------------------------|-------------|----------------|---|
| `Desktop` `DesktopDefault` | 32px        | 48px           | ✅ Breeze index.theme overrides C++ fallback |
| `Toolbar` `ToolbarDefault` | ⚙ 22px     | 22px           | ✅ |
| `MainToolbar` `MainToolbarDefault` | ⚙ 22px | 22px       | ✅ |
| `Small` `SmallDefault`     | ⚙ 16px     | 16px           | ✅ |
| `Panel` `PanelDefault`     | ⚙ 48px     | 48px           | ✅ Breeze index.theme matches C++ fallback (was 32 until KF5 v5.34.0, changed to 48 circa 2017) |
| `Dialog` `DialogDefault`   | ⚙ 32px     | 32px           | ✅ |

#### 1.3.7 Accessibility

| Setting              | Source                                       |   |
|----------------------|----------------------------------------------|---|
| Font DPI override    | Plasma 6.7: `~/.config/kcmfonts` `[General] forceFontDPI`, UInt, default 0 = unset, "Force font DPI on X11" ([fontsaasettingsbase.kcfg:8-11](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/blob/v6.7.5/kcms/fonts/fontsaasettingsbase.kcfg#L8-L11)); the fonts KCM shows it on X11 only (`visible: Qt.platform.pluginName === "xcb"`, [main.qml:424-426](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/blob/v6.7.5/kcms/fonts/ui/main.qml#L424-L426)), and krdb hands it to X11 as `Xft.dpi` only off Wayland ([krdb.cpp:328-341, :423-425](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/blob/v6.7.5/kcms/krdb/krdb.cpp#L328-L341)); the `plasma6.0-remove-dpi-settings` migration deletes `forceFontDPIWayland` ([kcms/fonts/kconf_update/plasma6.0-remove-dpi-settings.cpp:25](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/blob/v6.7.5/kcms/fonts/kconf_update/plasma6.0-remove-dpi-settings.cpp#L25)); the key is `kcmfonts`' alone, a file name without `rc`, since KConfig joins a name it is given to the config directory as it stands and adds `rc` only to an empty one ([kconfig.cpp:649, :652-678](https://invent.kde.org/frameworks/kconfig/-/blob/v6.31.0/src/core/kconfig.cpp#L652-L678), KConfig v6.31.0): the KCM binds its DPI setting to that file (`<kcfgfile name="kcmfonts" />`, [fontsaasettingsbase.kcfg:6](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/blob/v6.7.5/kcms/fonts/fontsaasettingsbase.kcfg#L6)) and krdb reads it there ([krdb.cpp:330, :339-340](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/blob/v6.7.5/kcms/krdb/krdb.cpp#L330-L340)), while the KCM's kdeglobals settings ([fontssettings.kcfg:6](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/blob/v6.7.5/kcms/fonts/fontssettings.kcfg#L6)) hold the fonts, not the DPI; no plasma-workspace or plasma-integration v6.7.5 source reads `forceFontDPI` from kdeglobals · Plasma 6.8: no `forceFontDPI` — the KCM's DPI setting and its kcfg entry are gone ([c675077b](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/commit/c675077b8f7371eb65bfa73c20d86a50828b13fc), first in v6.7.90), and so is krdb, its only reader ([fe367323](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/commit/fe367323b30bfa239243b2164d75faacb1be15e7)); `Xft.dpi` is `kwinrc [Xwayland] Scale` × 96 ([plasma-setup-xwayland/main.cpp:47-58](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/blob/v6.7.91/startkde/plasma-setup-xwayland/main.cpp#L47-L58)) | ✅ Plasma 6.7.5 (plasma-workspace `cc1ab796`); ✅ Plasma 6.8 at Beta 2 (v6.7.91, `40cde89a`), re-check at v6.8.0 (due 2026-10-14) |
| Scale factor         | Plasma 6.7 on X11: `forceFontDPI / 96` (from `kcmfonts`; 0 = unset) · Plasma 6.7 on Wayland: **(none)** — the KCM hides the DPI setting ("they should always be using the global scaling system instead", [main.qml:424-426](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/blob/v6.7.5/kcms/fonts/ui/main.qml#L424-L426)); krdb's `Xft.dpi` there is `kwinrc [Xwayland] Scale` × 96 ([krdb.cpp:333-337](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/blob/v6.7.5/kcms/krdb/krdb.cpp#L333-L337)), the Xwayland scale, not a text scale · Plasma 6.8: **(none)** — no `forceFontDPI` (Font DPI override above) | ✅ Plasma 6.7.5; ✅ Plasma 6.8 at Beta 2 (v6.7.91), re-check at v6.8.0 |
| Animation factor     | `kdeglobals [KDE] AnimationDurationFactor` (0 = disabled) | ✅ kwin.kcfg `<min>0</min>`; 0 is the intended "disabled" semantic |

### 1.4 GNOME

#### 1.4.1 Fonts

**gsettings keys:**

| Schema.Key                                          | Role         | Default (GNOME 48+)       | Pre-48 default     |   |
|-----------------------------------------------------|--------------|---------------------------|---------------------|---|
| `org.gnome.desktop.interface font-name`             | Body text    | ⚙ Adwaita Sans 11         | Cantarell 11        | ✅ |
| `org.gnome.desktop.interface document-font-name`    | Document text| ⚙ GNOME 48: Adwaita Sans 11 · GNOME 49+: Adwaita Sans 12 | Cantarell 11        | ✅ 12 since [87022e2b](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/gsettings-desktop-schemas/-/commit/87022e2b695f8fe3b95f3b8cfff9053855701470) "schemas: Increase document font size to 12" (first in 49.beta); [51.0 :158-159](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/gsettings-desktop-schemas/-/blob/51.0/schemas/org.gnome.desktop.interface.gschema.xml.in#L158-L159) |
| `org.gnome.desktop.interface monospace-font-name`   | Monospace    | ⚙ Adwaita Mono 11         | Source Code Pro 10  | ✅ |
| `org.gnome.desktop.wm.preferences titlebar-font`    | Title bar    | ⚙ Adwaita Sans Bold 11    | Cantarell Bold 11   | ✅ |

Font strings use Pango format: `[FAMILY-LIST] [STYLE-OPTIONS] SIZE [VARIATIONS] [FEATURES]` ✅
(e.g., "Cantarell Bold 11" → family=Cantarell, weight=Bold, size=11pt).
Style options can include weight, style (Italic), variant, stretch, gravity.
Optional `VARIATIONS` (e.g. `@wght=200`) and `FEATURES` (e.g. `#tnum=1`) segments are supported in modern Pango.

**libadwaita CSS type scale classes** (sizes are percentage-based,
shown here at default 11pt base):

All verified from libadwaita `src/stylesheet/widgets/_labels.scss`:

| CSS class         | CSS `font-size` | Computed size | Weight |   |
|-------------------|-----------------|---------------|--------|---|
| `.caption`        | 82%             | ≈ 9pt         | 400    | ✅ |
| `.caption-heading`| 82%             | ≈ 9pt         | 700    | ✅ |
| `.body`           | (inherited)     | (base font)   | 400    | ✅ |
| `.heading`        | (inherited)     | (base font)   | 700    | ✅ |
| `.title-4`        | 118%            | ≈ 13pt        | 700    | ✅ |
| `.title-3`        | 136%            | ≈ 15pt        | 700    | ✅ |
| `.title-2`        | 136%            | ≈ 15pt        | 800    | ✅ |
| `.title-1`        | 181%            | ≈ 20pt        | 800    | ✅ |

`.title-2` and `.title-3` intentionally share the same font-size (136%)
and are differentiated only by weight (800 vs 700). ✅

#### 1.4.2 Colors

**D-Bus portal (org.freedesktop.appearance):**

| Key            | What it provides                                            |   |
|----------------|-------------------------------------------------------------|---|
| `color-scheme` | Dark/light preference (0=no-preference, 1=prefer-dark, 2=prefer-light) | ✅ |
| `accent-color` | User-chosen accent hue (RGB doubles, out-of-range = unset)  | ✅ |
| `contrast`     | Contrast preference (0=normal, 1=high)                      | ✅ |
| `reduced-motion`| Motion preference (0=no-preference, 1=reduce)              | ✅ |

**libadwaita CSS** defines all other colors. The `adwaita` preset is
measured from these CSS values. GNOME provides no per-color system APIs
beyond the accent — everything comes from the CSS theme.

#### 1.4.3 Geometry

All geometry comes from **libadwaita CSS** (not system APIs):

| Property          | CSS source                     | Value  |   |
|-------------------|--------------------------------|--------|---|
| Control radius    | `$button_radius`               | 9px    | ✅ _common.scss |
| Card radius       | `$card_radius`                 | 12px   | ✅ _common.scss |
| Window/dialog radius | `$button_radius + 6`        | 15px   | ✅ for windows; AdwAlertDialog uses `$alert_radius: 18px` instead (see §1.4.4) |
| Frame/border width| libadwaita CSS `border-width`  | 1px    | ✅ |
| Focus ring width  | libadwaita CSS `outline-width` | 2px    | ✅ focus-ring mixin |
| Focus ring offset | libadwaita CSS `outline-offset`| -2px (inset) | ✅ `$offset: -$width` |
| Disabled opacity  | `--disabled-opacity`           | 0.5 (CSS: `50%`) | ✅ _colors.scss |
| Drop shadows      | libadwaita CSS `box-shadow`    | yes    | ✅ |

#### 1.4.5 Layout Spacing

libadwaita CSS defines specific per-widget margins and padding. There
is **no abstract spacing scale**. Specific values are set per CSS class.

#### 1.4.6 Icon Sizes

The active icon theme name is read from `org.gnome.desktop.interface
icon-theme` (default: `Adwaita`). ✅

GTK4 has **three** `GtkIconSize` enum values. Actual pixel sizes
come from theme CSS via `-gtk-icon-size`:

| `GtkIconSize`         | CSS class       | Adwaita default |   |
|-----------------------|-----------------|-----------------|---|
| `GTK_ICON_SIZE_INHERIT`| (parent)       | (inherited)     | ✅ |
| `GTK_ICON_SIZE_NORMAL`| `.normal-icons`  | 16px            | ✅ |
| `GTK_ICON_SIZE_LARGE` | `.large-icons`   | 32px            | ✅ |

Symbolic icons are designed at 16×16 SVG and rendered at 16, 32,
64, or 128px.

| Contrast preference  | Portal `org.freedesktop.appearance` `contrast`     | ✅ |
