## Chapter 2: Cross-Platform Property Mapping

Maps OS-specific APIs from Chapter 1 to unified per-widget properties.
Every visible style property of every widget is listed — including
properties whose value is inherited from a global default (`←`).

Each platform cell shows **where the default value comes from**: a
named API/constant, a CSS class, a measured value, `←` a global
property name, or `**(none)**` if the platform has no such concept. The Material column is Material 3 at pinned sources: the material-web token set v0_192 (the one its components use; `latest` names the 34.0.21 set) at material-web `cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3`, material-components-android 1.14.0 (`66c334b7946dabf33adfe1a2b7cad6bcaa4ea3ad`), and androidx Compose Material3 `1e6776853023ebe4266aca2b308305723e3a2d57`; 1 dp = 1 logical pixel, 1 rem = 16. Its cells say **(M3 token)** for an `md.comp` / `md.sys` token, **(impl)** for a material-web or Compose implementation constant cited at its pinned line (documented as a Breeze or libadwaita source constant is), **(derived)** for arithmetic on cited values, and **(app-defined)** where M3 leaves the value to the app; "L #…, D #…" is the light and dark baseline hex of a `md.sys.color` role, resolved through [v0_192/_md-sys-color.scss:19-135](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-sys-color.scss#L19-L135) and [v0_192/_md-ref-palette.scss:15-109](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-ref-palette.scss#L15-L109). The widget each table maps to: button = filled tonal button (primary = filled button), text input = outlined text field, tab bar = primary tabs, tooltip = plain tooltip, sidebar = standard navigation drawer, toolbar = small top app bar, combobox = outlined select, segmented control = outlined segmented button (deprecated in material-components-android 1.14.0), card = filled card.

#### Property naming conventions

Every property name is self-describing. The suffix/pattern tells you
the type and meaning:

**Colors and fills:**
- `*_color` — a color value (e.g. `border.color`, `caret_color`, `line_color`)
- `*_background` — a background fill color (e.g. `hover_background`, `checked_background`, `selection_background`). The base background (`background_color`) uses `_color` suffix for consistency with other global colors.
- `*_text_color` — a text rendering color for a specific state or context, kept outside the font struct because it overrides only the color, not the typeface (e.g. `active_text_color`, `disabled_text_color`, `hover_text_color`, `accent_text_color`)

**Typography:**
- `font` — a typeface struct: family + size + weight + style + **color**. Every text needs both a typeface and a color to render, so color is part of the font struct. When a widget inherits `font ← defaults.font`, it inherits the color too.
- `font.family`, `font.size`, `font.weight`, `font.style`, `font.color` — individual font sub-properties, shown when at least one platform has a widget-specific value. `font.style` is a string enum: `Normal` (upright), `Italic`, or `Oblique`. `font.color` is the default-state text rendering color for this font.

**Measurement rules:**

All **dimension and spacing** values are in **logical pixels**
(scale-independent) unless the property definition explicitly states a
different type. Non-pixel property types: ratios (`line_height`,
`disabled_opacity`, `border.opacity` — dimensionless multipliers),
booleans (`border.shadow_enabled`, `overlay_mode`, `underline_enabled`),
enums/strings (`button_order`, `font.style`, `icon_set`, `icon_theme`),
and colors (`*_color`, `*_background`). Unit suffixes in cells
("px", "pt", bare number) are informal annotations reflecting the
platform's native terminology — all dimension values are logical
pixels regardless of suffix. macOS "pt" = AppKit points = logical
pixels; Windows "px" / "epx" = effective pixels = logical pixels;
bare numbers are also logical pixels. Three general rules eliminate
ambiguity for every dimension and spacing property:

1. **Outer-box rule for dimensions:** `min_width`, `max_width`,
   `min_height`, `max_height`, `row_height`, `bar_height`,
   `segment_height`, `header_height`, and any other *height/width of a
   widget or element* measure the **outer bounding box** — from the
   outside of the border on one side to the outside of the border on
   the other side (border + padding + content). Drop shadows, focus
   rings, and any other visual effects that extend beyond the border
   edge are **not** included. When a platform's source value is a
   content-area measurement rather than outer-box, the cell shows the
   source value first and annotates the outer-box equivalent in
   parentheses — e.g. "CSS min-height: 24 (outer-box: 34)". The
   canonical property value is always the **outer-box** number.

2. **Per-side rule for padding:** `border.padding_horizontal` and
   `border.padding_vertical` are always **per-side** values — the
   amount applied to EACH side independently.
   `border.padding_horizontal: 10` means 10 px on the left AND 10 px
   on the right (20 px total horizontal gap). When a platform has
   asymmetric padding (different left vs right, or different top vs
   bottom), the cell shows both values (e.g. "10 left / 6 right").
   The model stores the four sides as separate fields —
   `border.padding_top`, `border.padding_right`, `border.padding_bottom`
   and `border.padding_left` (TOML `padding_top_px` … `padding_left_px`).
   A cell with one number sets both sides of its axis, which a preset
   may write with the TOML shorthand `padding_horizontal_px` or
   `padding_vertical_px`; an asymmetric cell sets each side to its own
   number, so "10 left / 6 right" is `padding_left_px = 10` and
   `padding_right_px = 6`, and "5 top / 6 bottom" is
   `padding_top_px = 5` and `padding_bottom_px = 6`. A side a cell does
   not state stays unstated.

3. **GNOME "sp" unit:** Some GNOME/libadwaita values are suffixed
   "sp" (e.g. "300sp"). This is libadwaita's scale-independent pixel
   unit — it equals logical pixels at the default text scale factor
   (1.0) and scales proportionally with
   `org.gnome.desktop.interface text-scaling-factor`. For our
   purposes, **sp = logical pixels** (the text-scaling-factor is
   applied globally by the toolkit, not per-property by us).

**Border struct** (`border.*` sub-properties):

The border struct groups all frame/boundary properties of a widget.
Like `font`, it can appear as a single inherited row
(`border = ← defaults.border`) or expanded into sub-properties when
values differ per platform.

- `border.line_width` — stroke thickness of the border outline.
- `border.corner_radius` — corner rounding radius. `border.corner_radius_lg` is the larger variant used by popover/window/dialog containers.
- `border.color` — color of the border outline.
- `border.opacity` — the platform's line opacity: the share of the text colour in a line, already folded into every stated `border.color`, which is the final line colour. No consumer multiplies a colour by it.
- `border.shadow_enabled` — whether the widget casts a drop shadow.
- `border.padding_horizontal` — per-side left/right space between the inner border edge and the widget's content (text, icon).
- `border.padding_vertical` — per-side top/bottom space between the inner border edge and the widget's content.
- In the model these two rows are four side fields, `border.padding_top`, `border.padding_right`, `border.padding_bottom` and `border.padding_left`; see conventions point 2 for how a cell maps to them.

`defaults.border` provides: `line_width`, `corner_radius`,
`corner_radius_lg`, `color`, `opacity`, `shadow_enabled`. Padding has
no global default — it is always widget-specific.

**Content gaps and layout margins:**
- `icon_text_gap` — horizontal distance between an icon and the adjacent text label inside the widget.
- `label_gap` — distance between an indicator (checkbox/radio box) and its text label.
- `item_gap` — distance between adjacent child items in a container (toolbar items, etc.).
- `button_gap` — distance between adjacent action buttons (e.g. OK / Cancel in a dialog).
- `widget_gap` — default distance between sibling widgets in a layout container.
- `section_gap` — vertical distance between content sections.
- `container_margin` — default margin inside a nested layout container.
- `window_margin` — default margin inside a top-level window layout.

**Widget dimensions:**
- `min_width`, `max_width` — minimum/maximum outer width of the widget (see outer-box rule).
- `min_height`, `max_height` — minimum/maximum outer height of the widget (see outer-box rule).
- `row_height` — height of a single item row (menu item, list row).
- `bar_height` — total height of a toolbar bar.
- `track_height` — height of a slider or progress bar track groove.
- `track_width` — width of a switch track.
- `track_radius` — corner radius of the switch track. A value of half the `track_height` produces a pill/capsule shape.
- `thumb_diameter` — diameter of the circular slider/switch thumb knob.
- `thumb_width` — width of the scrollbar thumb element.
- `min_thumb_length` — minimum length of the scrollbar thumb along the scroll axis.
- `groove_width` — total width of the scrollbar groove (track area + margins).
- `divider_width` — width of the splitter divider handle area.
- `line_width` — stroke thickness of a separator line.
- `indicator_width` — side length of the checkbox indicator (square) or diameter of the radio indicator (circle); `radio_dot_diameter` — diameter of the filled dot a selected radio button draws at the centre of that circle.
- `arrow_icon_size` — size (width = height) of a dropdown arrow icon.
- `arrow_area_width` — total width of the clickable dropdown arrow area including its surrounding padding.
- `stroke_width` — stroke thickness of the spinner ring arc.
- `diameter` — default diameter of the spinner ring.
- `min_diameter` — minimum allowed spinner diameter.
- `segment_height` — height of each segment button in a segmented control.
- `separator_width` — width of the divider line between segments.
- `header_height` — height of an expander header row.
- `tick_mark_length` — length of slider tick marks, measured perpendicular to the track.

**Other dimensions:**
- `disabled_opacity` — opacity multiplier (0.0–1.0) applied to the entire widget when disabled. A consumer applies both it and the `disabled_*` colours; a platform uses one mechanism, so the data makes the other an identity: 1.0 where the platform dims by colour (KDE, macOS, Windows), and disabled colours equal to the normal ones (or unstated) where it dims by opacity (GNOME).
- `icon_size` — display size (width = height) of icons within the widget.

**Dimensionless ratios:**
- `line_height` — ratio of line box height to font size (e.g. 1.19 means a 13px font produces ~15.5px line boxes). Typically derived from font metrics (sTypoAscender + |sTypoDescender| + sTypoLineGap) / unitsPerEm; Windows uses the Fluent type ramp value instead (Body 20px / 14px). **Not** in pixels — multiply by `font.size` to get the pixel line height.
- `border.opacity` — 0.0–1.0, the text colour's share in a line, already folded into `border.color`; not pixels, and not applied again.

**Booleans and enums:**
- `border.shadow_enabled` — whether the widget renders a drop shadow.
- `overlay_mode` — boolean: `true` if the scrollbar uses overlay (auto-hiding) mode, `false` if persistent/legacy. Platform cells show how to query this (e.g. `NSScroller.preferredScrollerStyle`).
- `underline_enabled` — whether link text is underlined.
- `button_order` — platform convention for dialog button arrangement (primary left vs right).
- `icon_set` — which icon set to use.
- `icon_theme` — which icon theme to use.

**Named fonts (for widgets with multiple text areas):**
- `body_font` — typeface for the primary body text (e.g. dialog message body). Includes `body_font.color`.
- `title_font.*` — typeface sub-properties for the title/heading text. Includes `title_font.color`.
- `title_bar_font.*` — typeface sub-properties for the window title bar text. Includes `title_bar_font.color`.
- `item_font` — typeface for list/table row content text. Includes `item_font.color`.
- `header_font` — typeface for list/table column header text. Includes `header_font.color`.

**Text scale roles (§2.19 only):**
- `caption`, `section_heading`, `dialog_title`, `display` — these are
  content role names, not widget properties. Each maps a semantic text
  role to per-platform type ramp entries (size + weight).

### 2.1 Global Defaults

#### 2.1.1 Base Font

| Property       | macOS                               | Windows                                | KDE                        | GNOME                       | Material |
|----------------|-------------------------------------|----------------------------------------|----------------------------|-----------------------------|---|
| `family`       | `+systemFontOfSize:` → family · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :30](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L30)) | `lfMessageFont.lfFaceName`          | `[General] font` field 0 | `font-name` gsetting      | **(M3 token)** `md.ref.typeface.plain` = Roboto ([v0_192/_md-ref-typeface.scss:18](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-ref-typeface.scss#L18)) |
| `size`         | `+systemFontOfSize:` → pointSize · macOS 26: same, 13 ([probe 26 :32](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L32)) | ↕ `abs(lfMessageFont.lfHeight)*72/dpi`: 9pt (lfHeight −12) at 96 DPI on the three Windows probe images (§1.2.1); **(Fluent)** WinUI3 controls draw at `ControlContentThemeFontSize` = 14px = 10.5pt ([generic.xaml:3961, :36](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/dxaml/xcp/dxaml/themes/generic.xaml#L3961)), the preset's size | `[General] font` field 1 | `font-name` gsetting → size | **(M3 token)** `md.sys.typescale.body-medium-size` 0.875rem = 14 ([v0_192/_md-sys-typescale.scss:56](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-sys-typescale.scss#L56)) |
| `weight`       | `NSFontDescriptor` traits · macOS 26: same, 400 ([probe 26 :33](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L33)) | `lfMessageFont.lfWeight`            | `[General] font` field 4 | `font-name` gsetting → wt | **(M3 token)** `body-medium-weight` = `weight-regular` 400 ([v0_192/_md-sys-typescale.scss:58](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-sys-typescale.scss#L58); [v0_192/_md-ref-typeface.scss:21](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-ref-typeface.scss#L21)) |

`defaults.text_color` and `defaults.font.color` are the same value
(both resolve to the primary text color). `defaults.text_color` is kept
here as a standalone color token so that state-override properties
(`hover_text_color`, `disabled_text_color`, `active_text_color`, etc.)
have a clear reference. Per-widget base text colors use `font.color`.

macOS provides separate APIs for content/row selection
(`selectedContentBackgroundColor` + `alternateSelectedControlTextColor`)
and text-range selection (`selectedTextBackgroundColor` +
`selectedTextColor`). Other platforms use the same colors for both
contexts. `text_selection_*` tokens expose this distinction — widgets
with editable text (§2.4) inherit `text_selection_*`, while list/sidebar
widgets (§2.12, §2.15) inherit `selection_*`.

| `info_color`                | `systemBlueColor` · macOS 26: same API ([probe 26 :346](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L346), :420) | **(Fluent)** `SystemFillColorAttentionBrush` — L `SystemAccentColor`, D `SystemAccentColorLight2` ([Common_themeresources_any.xaml:369, :165](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Common_themeresources_any.xaml#L369)), from `UISettings` `Accent` / `AccentLight2`: #0078d4 / #4cc2ff on Windows 11 25H2 and Server 2025 ([probe.toml:756, :758](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L756)) | `[Colors:View] ForegroundActive`     | **(Adwaita CSS)** none named — libadwaita 1.10.0 defines no info colour; the deprecated GtkInfoBar draws its `info` type from `--accent-bg-color` ([_deprecated.scss:174](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/stylesheet/widgets/_deprecated.scss#L174)), the accent, default `#3584e4` (adw-accent-color.c:53-54)  | **(none)** — no info role |
| `info_text_color`     | `labelColor` ¹ · macOS 26: same | **(Fluent)** `InfoBarInformationalSeverityIconForeground` = `TextFillColorInverseBrush` — L #FFFFFF, D #E4000000 ² ([InfoBar_themeresources.xaml:16, :35](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/InfoBar/InfoBar_themeresources.xaml#L16); [Common_themeresources_any.xaml:213, :9](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Common_themeresources_any.xaml#L213)): the glyph InfoBar draws on its `SystemFillColorAttention` icon fill (:12) | `[Colors:Window] ForegroundNormal` ¹ | **(Adwaita CSS)** ² | **(none)** |

**Status foreground semantic mismatch:** The `*_text_color` rows mix two
different concepts across platforms. ¹ macOS and KDE provide the
**normal body foreground** — suitable as text color *alongside* a status
indicator (e.g. error-message text next to a red icon), **not** as text
*on* a status-colored background. ² Windows and GNOME provide a **contrast
foreground for text on the status background** (Windows: white-on-dark-red in light
mode, near-black-on-light-pink in dark mode; GNOME: libadwaita 1.10.0's `destructive_fg_color`, `warning_fg_color`, `success_fg_color` ([_colors.scss:13, :20, :17](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/stylesheet/_colors.scss#L13)) and, for info, `accent_fg_color` (adw-style-manager.c:161): white, warning's `RGB(0 0 0 / 80%)`). No platform has a dedicated
"text-on-status-background" API; consumers must pick the interpretation
that matches their use case and derive the other (e.g. ensure contrast
against the `danger_color` color if using it as a fill).

### 2.2 Window / Application Chrome

| Property                 | macOS                                         | Windows                                         | KDE                              | GNOME                                            | Material |
|--------------------------|-----------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------|---|
| `background_color`             | ← `defaults.background_color`                      | ← `defaults.background_color`                         | ← `defaults.background_color`         | ← `defaults.background_color`                         | ← `defaults.background_color` (`surface`) |

### 2.5 Checkbox / Radio Button

| Property        | macOS                     | Windows                                      | KDE                                   | GNOME                    | Material |
|-----------------|---------------------------|----------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------|--------------------------|---|
| `background_color`    | **(measured)** white · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value | **(Fluent)** `CheckBoxCheckBackgroundFillUnchecked` = `ControlAltFillColorSecondaryBrush` — L #06000000, D #19000000 ([CheckBox_themeresources.xaml:229, :53](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/CheckBox_themeresources.xaml#L229); [Common_themeresources_any.xaml:234, :30](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Common_themeresources_any.xaml#L234)) | `[Colors:Button] BackgroundNormal` | **(Adwaita CSS)** check bg| **(M3 token)** unchecked: none. `comp.checkbox` has no unselected container colour, only an outline ([v0_192/_md-comp-checkbox.scss:118-120](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-comp-checkbox.scss#L118-L120)), so the box is transparent. |

| `unchecked_border_color` | ← `border.color` — the measured gray outline of that row · macOS 26: none **(measured)**: the 26.6.2 render's unchecked box has no edge pixel that differs from its fill, only antialiased corners ([render](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/renders/aqua/checkbox-regular-off@2x.png)); the inactive-window caveat of `unchecked_background` | **(Fluent)** `CheckBoxCheckBackgroundStrokeUnchecked` = `ControlStrongStrokeColorDefaultBrush` — L #72000000, D #8BFFFFFF ([CheckBox_themeresources.xaml:217, :41](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/CheckBox_themeresources.xaml#L217)), the `border.color` row's brush | **(Breeze src)** `separatorColor()` = `KColorUtils::mix(Window, WindowText, frameContrast)`, the unchecked box's pen ([breezehelper.cpp:855-858](https://github.com/KDE/breeze/blob/f0b1d7534aa2356d7336241d0c7051522e8a6b68/kstyle/breezehelper.cpp#L855-L858), :329-332) — Breeze #c6c8c9, Breeze Dark #4c4e51 (the `border.color` row) | **(Adwaita CSS)** `$trough_color` = `color-mix(in srgb, currentColor 15%, transparent)`, drawn as a 2px inset `box-shadow` ring ([_checks.scss:27](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/stylesheet/widgets/_checks.scss#L27); [_colors.scss:280](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/stylesheet/_colors.scss#L280)) | **(M3 token)** `unselected-outline-color` = `on-surface-variant` — L #49454f, D #cac4d0, width 2 ([v0_192/_md-comp-checkbox.scss:118-120](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-comp-checkbox.scss#L118-L120)) |
> Radio buttons use the same colors but with circular `border.corner_radius`; on KDE those are Breeze's text-colour mark on a tinted fill above, which its radio draws as its checkbox does (breezehelper.cpp:995-1024, :1059).

[ToolTipService_Partial.cpp:1731-1783](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/dxaml/xcp/dxaml/lib/ToolTipService_Partial.cpp#L1731-L1783)), and 400 when the call fails (`DEFAULT_SPI_GETMOUSEHOVERTIME`, [ToolTipService_Partial.h:17-19](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/dxaml/xcp/dxaml/lib/ToolTipService_Partial.h#L17-L19)); **(API)** `SPI_GETMOUSEHOVERTIME` is 400 on the three Windows probe images ([probe.toml:703](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-14-run-38478334574/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L703)) | 700 **(impl)** — Qt's `m_toolTipWakeUpDelay{700}` ([qstylehints_p.h:45](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/d865bf9d4877fa09e1fc02e2ad14fb9fd43ef430/src/gui/kernel/qstylehints_p.h#L45)), which `QCommonStyle` returns for `SH_ToolTip_WakeUpDelay` ([qcommonstyle.cpp:5351-5356](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/d865bf9d4877fa09e1fc02e2ad14fb9fd43ef430/src/widgets/styles/qcommonstyle.cpp#L5351-L5356)); Qt v6.10.0 states 700 there directly ([qcommonstyle.cpp:5375-5380](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/v6.10.0/src/widgets/styles/qcommonstyle.cpp#L5375-L5380)); Kirigami's `Units.toolTipDelay` is 700 too ([platform/units.cpp:45](https://invent.kde.org/frameworks/kirigami/-/blob/8319acc7dd1149664147ec1e22c39ae6143ed752/src/platform/units.cpp#L45)) | 500 **(impl)** — `HOVER_TIMEOUT` ([gtktooltip.c:71-73](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/gtk/-/blob/4.24.1/gtk/gtktooltip.c#L71-L73), used at :827) | 0 — shown on pointer enter **(impl)**: the tooltip anchor calls `state.show` on `PointerEventType.Enter` ([compose/internal/BasicTooltip.kt:271-276](https://github.com/androidx/androidx/blob/1e6776853023ebe4266aca2b308305723e3a2d57/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/internal/BasicTooltip.kt#L271-L276)), and `TooltipState.show` waits for nothing ([compose/Tooltip.kt:1075-1099](https://github.com/androidx/androidx/blob/1e6776853023ebe4266aca2b308305723e3a2d57/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Tooltip.kt#L1075-L1099)) |

`max_width` measures the tooltip's **outer bounding box** width in
logical pixels (per the outer-box rule) — from the outside of the
border on one side to the outside of the border on the other side.
It does **not** measure text character count. Text that exceeds this
width wraps to multiple lines. macOS value is measured from rendered
tooltips; WinUI3 value is a XAML `MaxWidth` property; KDE and GNOME
have no platform limit — preset values are our defaults.

### 2.12 Sidebar
| Property     | macOS                      | Windows                | KDE                                      | GNOME                   | Material |
|--------------|----------------------------|------------------------|------------------------------------------|-------------------------|---|
| `background_color` | `NSVisualEffectView.Material.sidebar`, "the material for the background of window sidebars" (macOS 10.11+, [docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nsvisualeffectview/material-swift.enum/sidebar.json), read 2026-10-11): a material, not a colour; no colour value ❓ · macOS 26: the same material ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nsvisualeffectview/material-swift.enum/sidebar.json), read 2026-10-11); the HIG: sidebars "can float above content in the Liquid Glass layer" ([HIG sidebars](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/design/human-interface-guidelines/sidebars.json), read 2026-10-11); | `background_color`   | ← `defaults.background_color`   | ← `defaults.background_color`   | **(Breeze src)** the Header set's `[Colors:Header] BackgroundNormal` — Breeze #dee0e2, Breeze Dark #292c30 ([BreezeLight.colors:59](https://github.com/KDE/breeze/blob/f0b1d7534aa2356d7336241d0c7051522e8a6b68/colors/BreezeLight.colors#L59); BreezeDark.colors:60): Breeze fills a `QMainWindow`'s tools area, its menu bar and top toolbars, with the tools-area palette's `Window`, the Header set ([breezestyle.cpp:1322, :1341-1347](https://github.com/KDE/breeze/blob/f0b1d7534aa2356d7336241d0c7051522e8a6b68/kstyle/breezestyle.cpp#L1341-L1347); [breezetoolsareamanager.cpp:201-215](https://github.com/KDE/breeze/blob/f0b1d7534aa2356d7336241d0c7051522e8a6b68/kstyle/breezetoolsareamanager.cpp#L201-L215)), where the scheme has Header colours (:232; both Breeze schemes do, BreezeLight.colors:57) and the window decoration's border size is automatic, None or NoSides ([breezehelper.cpp:1904-1941](https://github.com/KDE/breeze/blob/f0b1d7534aa2356d7336241d0c7051522e8a6b68/kstyle/breezehelper.cpp#L1904-L1941)); an inactive window takes `[Colors:Header][Inactive]` (BreezeLight.colors:73, BreezeDark.colors:74). Otherwise the toolbar draws no fill of its own (`border.color` below) and shows the window's, ← `defaults.background_color` | ← `defaults.background_color` | **(M3 token)** `container-color` = `surface` ([v0_192/_md-comp-top-app-bar-small.scss:34](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-comp-top-app-bar-small.scss#L34)); on scroll `surface-container` (:60). Docked toolbar: `surface-container` ([latest/_md-comp-toolbar-docked.scss:34](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/versions/latest/sass/_md-comp-toolbar-docked.scss#L34)). |
| `icon_size`    | default=32, small mode=24 (`NSToolbar.SizeMode`, deprecated) — see §2.1.8 toolbar · macOS 26: same; deprecated in 27 (§1.1.6) | WinUI3: 16 — see §2.1.8 toolbar | 22 — see §2.1.8 toolbar | 16 — see §2.1.8 toolbar | **(M3 token)** 24 ([v0_192/_md-comp-top-app-bar-small.scss:59](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-comp-top-app-bar-small.scss#L59), :65) |
| `border.color` | ❓ — the toolbar's lower edge is the window's title-bar separator, `NSWindow.titlebarSeparatorStyle`, whose default is `.automatic`, "A style indicating that the system determines the type of separator" (macOS 11+, [docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nswindow/titlebarseparatorstyle.json), [NSTitlebarSeparatorStyle](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nstitlebarseparatorstyle.json), read 2026-10-17); no colour is published · macOS 26: ❓ — the same API, no colour | **(none)** at rest: a closed `CommandBar` draws no frame. Its only one, `OpenBorder`, is collapsed (`Visibility="Collapsed"`, [CommandBar_themeresources.xaml:842](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/CommandBar_themeresources.xaml#L842)) until an open state shows it (:562-564; the default `CompactClosed` sets nothing, :553); open, it is `CommandBarBorderBrushOpen` = `CardStrokeColorDefaultSolidBrush` (:55, :11) — L #EBEBEB, D #1C1C1C ([Common_themeresources_any.xaml:251, :47](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Common_themeresources_any.xaml#L251)) | **(Breeze src)** the bottom edge only, in the Header set's `separatorColor()` = `KColorUtils::mix(Header bg, Header fg, frameContrast)`: Breeze draws no toolbar frame (a QToolBar's `CE_ToolBar` is `emptyControl`, [breezestyle.cpp:1466-1467](https://github.com/KDE/breeze/blob/f0b1d7534aa2356d7336241d0c7051522e8a6b68/kstyle/breezestyle.cpp#L1466-L1467); [breezestyle.h:297-300](https://github.com/KDE/breeze/blob/f0b1d7534aa2356d7336241d0c7051522e8a6b68/kstyle/breezestyle.h#L297-L300)), but it strokes a line along the bottom of a `QMainWindow`'s tools area, its menu bar and top toolbars (`drawWidgetPrimitive`, breezestyle.cpp:1349-1350), in the tools area's palette, the Header set ([breezetoolsareamanager.cpp:201-215](https://github.com/KDE/breeze/blob/f0b1d7534aa2356d7336241d0c7051522e8a6b68/kstyle/breezetoolsareamanager.cpp#L201-L215)) — Breeze #b9bbbd, Breeze Dark #535659 (Header #dee0e2 / #232629, #292c30 / #fcfcfc, [BreezeLight.colors:59, :67](https://github.com/KDE/breeze/blob/f0b1d7534aa2356d7336241d0c7051522e8a6b68/colors/BreezeLight.colors#L59); BreezeDark.colors:60, :68; computed with KF 6.30.0 `KColorUtils::mix`). This column follows Breeze's Qt Widgets style, as its other §2.13 cells do; qqc2-desktop-style's QML `ToolBar` draws a `Kirigami.Separator` there instead | **(none)** — `.toolbar` and the `%toolbar` it extends set no border ([_toolbars.scss:1-110](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/stylesheet/widgets/_toolbars.scss#L1-L110)); an `AdwToolbarView` top bar in its `.raised` style draws a 1px shade line below it, `color-mix(in srgb, var(--headerbar-shade-color) 50%, transparent)` (:314-316) | **(none)** — `comp.top-app-bar-small` has no outline or divider token ([v0_192/_md-comp-top-app-bar-small.scss:33-66](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-comp-top-app-bar-small.scss#L33-L66)) |
| `border.corner_radius` | ❓ — not published · macOS 26: ❓ — the HIG has toolbar controls' "corner radii that are concentric with bar corners" and gives the bar's none ([HIG toolbars](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/design/human-interface-guidelines/toolbars.json), read 2026-10-17) | 0 at rest — the style's `CornerRadius` = `ControlCornerRadius` 4 (CommandBar_themeresources.xaml:89; [CornerRadius_themeresources.xaml:5](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/CornerRadius_themeresources.xaml#L5)) reaches the bar's `ContentRoot` only in the open states (CommandBar_themeresources.xaml:559-560; `ContentRoot`, :795) | **(none)** — no frame (`border.color`) | **(none)** — `.toolbar` sets no radius; only a stand-alone `.toolbar.osd` is rounded, `$button_radius + 6px` = 15 ([_toolbars.scss:99-110](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/stylesheet/widgets/_toolbars.scss#L99-L110); [_common.scss:8](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/stylesheet/_common.scss#L8)) | **(M3 token)** 0 — `container-shape` = `corner-none` ([v0_192/_md-comp-top-app-bar-small.scss:37](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-comp-top-app-bar-small.scss#L37)) |

### 2.14 Status Bar

| Property      | macOS              | Windows                               | KDE                | GNOME              | Material |
|---------------|--------------------|---------------------------------------|--------------------|--------------------|---|
| `font.family` | ← `defaults.font` | `lfStatusFont.lfFaceName`          | ← `defaults.font` | ← `defaults.font` | **(none)** — M3 has no status bar. `comp.bottom-app-bar` is an action bar, not a status bar. |
| `font.size`   | ← `defaults.font` | ↕ `abs(lfStatusFont.lfHeight)*72/dpi` | ← `defaults.font` | ← `defaults.font` | **(none)** |
| `font.weight` | ← `defaults.font` | `lfStatusFont.lfWeight`            | ← `defaults.font` | ← `defaults.font` | **(none)** |
| `font.style`  | ← `defaults.font` | `lfStatusFont.lfItalic` (0 = Normal) | ← `defaults.font` | ← `defaults.font` | **(none)** |
| `font.color`  | ← `defaults.font.color` | ← `defaults.font.color`        | ← `defaults.font.color` | ← `defaults.font.color` | **(none)** |
| `background_color`  | ← `defaults.background_color` | ← `defaults.background_color`        | ← `defaults.background_color` | ← `defaults.background_color` | **(none)** |
| `border.color`      | ← `defaults.border.color` | ← `defaults.border.color` | ← `defaults.border.color` | ← `defaults.border.color` | **(none)** |
| `border.line_width` | ← `defaults.border.line_width` | ← `defaults.border.line_width` | ← `defaults.border.line_width` | ← `defaults.border.line_width` | **(none)** |
| `border.corner_radius` | **(none)** — rectangular bar · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value | **(none)** — rectangular bar | **(none)** — rectangular bar | **(none)** — rectangular bar | **(none)** |
| `border.shadow_enabled` | **(none)** — no shadow · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value | **(none)** — no shadow | **(none)** — no shadow | **(none)** — no shadow | **(none)** |
| `border.padding_horizontal` | **(none)** — no window status-bar widget: AppKit's `NSStatusBar` manages status items "displayed within the system-wide menu bar" ([Apple docs](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsstatusbar)); HIG Status bars: "Not supported in macOS" ([HIG](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/status-bars)) · macOS 26: same ([HIG status-bars](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/design/human-interface-guidelines/status-bars.json), read 2026-10-11) | **(none)** — not specified: Win32 status bar borders are runtime-queryable **(API)** ([`SB_GETBORDERS`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/controls/sb-getborders)), no default documented ([Status Bars](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/controls/status-bars)); WinUI3 has no status-bar control ([controls list](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/controls/)) | ✅ 2 left / 14 right — Qt `QStatusBar::reformat()`: layout margins `QMargins()` (all 0), `addSpacing(2)` before the items, nothing after them ([qstatusbar.cpp:418-474](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/d865bf9d4877fa09e1fc02e2ad14fb9fd43ef430/src/widgets/widgets/qstatusbar.cpp#L418-L474)); then the size grip, enabled by default (:200-201; KXmlGui leaves it so), 1px after the items (:466-467) and 13px wide: its size hint is `QSize(13, 13)` through `CT_SizeGrip` ([qsizegrip.cpp:198-202](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/d865bf9d4877fa09e1fc02e2ad14fb9fd43ef430/src/widgets/widgets/qsizegrip.cpp#L198-L202)), horizontal policy `Minimum` (:183), and neither Breeze (`sizeFromContents` → parent, [breezestyle.cpp:1155-1156](https://github.com/KDE/breeze/blob/f0b1d7534aa2356d7336241d0c7051522e8a6b68/kstyle/breezestyle.cpp#L1155-L1156)) nor `KStyle` ([kstyle.cpp](https://invent.kde.org/frameworks/frameworkintegration/-/blob/178c522c70c0c1295f59d4776596c8a361dec872/src/kstyle/kstyle.cpp), no `sizeFromContents`) changes it, `QCommonStyle` returning it as given ([qcommonstyle.cpp:5032-5035](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/d865bf9d4877fa09e1fc02e2ad14fb9fd43ef430/src/widgets/styles/qcommonstyle.cpp#L5032-L5035)). Breeze paints the grip as nothing (`CE_SizeGrip` → `emptyControl`, [breezestyle.cpp:1505-1506](https://github.com/KDE/breeze/blob/f0b1d7534aa2356d7336241d0c7051522e8a6b68/kstyle/breezestyle.cpp#L1505-L1506)), so the items end 1 + 13 = 14px of empty bar before the right edge. Context: a window that is neither maximized nor full screen; in those two states the grip is hidden (qsizegrip.cpp:76-89, :450-463) and the right is 0. `showMessage()` text: 6 left / 12 right (`messageRect()`, :97-98). Breeze has no status-bar metric (`breezemetrics.h`) | ✅ **(Adwaita CSS)** 10 — `statusbar { padding: 6px 10px 6px 10px; }` ([_deprecated.scss:197-199](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/a5030fc692855c62a0014ea784d3a80ce30f926f/src/stylesheet/widgets/_deprecated.scss#L197-199)); `GtkStatusbar` "Deprecated: 4.10" ([gtkstatusbar.c:83](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/gtk/-/blob/57f569a23cc23402eb122e337b69d57e4aeecd2b/gtk/deprecated/gtkstatusbar.c#L83)) | **(none)** |
| `border.padding_vertical` | **(none)** — no window status-bar widget (see `border.padding_horizontal`) · macOS 26: same (as `border.padding_horizontal`) | **(none)** — not specified (see `border.padding_horizontal`) | ✅ 3 top / 2 bottom — Qt `QStatusBar::reformat()`: `addSpacing(3)` above the items row, `addSpacing(2)` below it (qstatusbar.cpp:437, :472). Breeze's 1px top separator is painted inside the bar, not laid out (`drawPanelStatusBarPrimitive`) | ✅ **(Adwaita CSS)** 6 — same `statusbar` rule | **(none)** |

Tree views share List/Table properties (row height, padding, selection,
hover) plus tree-specific layout: per-level indentation width and
expand/collapse arrow column. These are toolkit-internal (Qt
`QTreeView::indentation()` default=20, GTK `GtkTreeExpander` arrow=16px,
AppKit `NSOutlineView.indentationPerLevel` default=13, macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9: [probe 14 :504](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L504), [probe 15 :504](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-15/probe.toml#L504)) and not exposed
as theme properties — they inherit the list row styling above.

### 2.16 Popover / Dropdown

### 2.20 Layout Container Defaults

Default spacing for toolkit layout containers (`QLayout`, `NSStackView`,
`GtkBox`, `StackPanel`). These are the values a layout manager uses when
the developer does not specify explicit spacing. None of these are
user-configurable settings — they are compile-time constants (KDE
`breezemetrics.h`), design guidelines (macOS HIG), or hardcoded CSS
(GNOME). Windows has no layout container defaults; `StackPanel.Spacing`
defaults to 0 and apps pick from the Fluent token ramp (§1.2.5)
themselves.

| Property           | macOS HIG            | Windows Fluent                | KDE Breeze                        | GNOME libadwaita       | Material |
|--------------------|----------------------|-------------------------------|-----------------------------------|------------------------|---|
| `widget_gap`       | 8 **(HIG)** · macOS 26: same ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nsstackview/spacing.json), read 2026-10-11) | **(none)** — app chooses from Fluent ramp | `Layout_DefaultSpacing` = 6       | 6 **(measured)**       | **(none)** — M3 has no layout-manager defaults in either token set |
| `container_margin` | **(none)** — not specified · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value | **(none)**                    | `Layout_ChildMarginWidth` = 6     | 12 **(measured)**      | **(none)** |
| `window_margin`    | 20 **(HIG)** · macOS 26: same as §1.1.5's content margin, the Auto Layout Guide's 20 (unversioned) | **(none)**                    | `Layout_TopLevelMarginWidth` = 10 | 12 **(measured)**      | **(none)** — not in any token set. The m3.material.io layout pages that give window margins could not be read (they render only with JavaScript). |
| `section_gap`      | 20 **(HIG)** · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value | **(none)**                    | **(none)** — not specified        | 18 **(measured)**      | **(none)**. For reference only: Compose adaptive panes put a 24 spacer between panes at expanded width and wider, 0 at compact and medium ([compose-adaptive/layout/PaneScaffoldDirective.kt:56-74](https://github.com/androidx/androidx/blob/1e6776853023ebe4266aca2b308305723e3a2d57/compose/material3/adaptive/adaptive-layout/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/adaptive/layout/PaneScaffoldDirective.kt#L56-L74)) |

These are platform layout-manager defaults — what the toolkit uses when
the developer does not specify explicit spacing. Same pattern as
per-widget spacing fields like `dialog.border.padding_horizontal` or
`toolbar.item_gap`.

macOS NSSwitch introduced in 10.15. KDE has no QWidget toggle — only QQC2/Kirigami `Switch` with font-metric-derived sizing.

Note: KWin's default-on Scale effect (0.8 → 1, 200 ms; [scale/package/contents/code/main.js:35-80](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/kwin/-/blob/v6.7.5/src/plugins/scale/package/contents/code/main.js#L35-L80), [metadata.json:93](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/kwin/-/blob/v6.7.5/src/plugins/scale/package/metadata.json#L93)) animates toplevel windows such as a `QDialog`, not an in-window dialog, and Qt's [qdialog.cpp](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/d865bf9d4877fa09e1fc02e2ad14fb9fd43ef430/src/widgets/dialogs/qdialog.cpp) animates nothing | `scale_fade` **(impl)** — the floating `AdwDialog`'s sheet fades in while its scale rises from `MIN_SCALE` 0.8 to 1 on a spring ([adw-floating-sheet.c:22, :34-35, :101, :220-226](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/adw-floating-sheet.c#L22)); with reduced motion it only fades (:482) | `scale_fade` **(impl)** — material-components-android's M3 dialog theme overlays set `android:windowAnimationStyle` to `MaterialAlertDialog.Material3.Animation` ([mdc/themes_overlay.xml:73, :119](https://github.com/material-components/material-components-android/blob/66c334b7946dabf33adfe1a2b7cad6bcaa4ea3ad/lib/java/com/google/android/material/dialog/res/values/themes_overlay.xml#L73)), whose enter animation is `m3_motion_fade_enter` ([mdc/styles.xml:113-116](https://github.com/material-components/material-components-android/blob/66c334b7946dabf33adfe1a2b7cad6bcaa4ea3ad/lib/java/com/google/android/material/dialog/res/values/styles.xml#L113-L116)): alpha 0 → 1 with scale 0.8 → 1 about the centre ([mdc/m3_motion_fade_enter.xml:17-31](https://github.com/material-components/material-components-android/blob/66c334b7946dabf33adfe1a2b7cad6bcaa4ea3ad/lib/java/com/google/android/material/motion/res/anim/m3_motion_fade_enter.xml#L17-L31)), 400 ms (`m3_sys_motion_duration_medium4`, [mdc/tokens.xml:129](https://github.com/material-components/material-components-android/blob/66c334b7946dabf33adfe1a2b7cad6bcaa4ea3ad/lib/java/com/google/android/material/motion/res/values/tokens.xml#L129)). Compose's `AlertDialog.kt` defines none: `BasicAlertDialog` opens a plain `Dialog` ([compose/AlertDialog.kt:136-142](https://github.com/androidx/androidx/blob/1e6776853023ebe4266aca2b308305723e3a2d57/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/AlertDialog.kt#L136-L142)). Note: material-web's dialog slides down instead (`translateY(-50px)` → 0, 500 ms; [mw/dialog/internal/animations.ts:52-104](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/dialog/internal/animations.ts#L52-L104)), not taken as the value |

Dialog dimensions (`min_width`, `max_width`, `min_height`, `max_height`)
measure the **dialog surface** — the visible dialog box from its outer
border edge to outer border edge. This includes the title area,
`border.padding_*`, body text area, and button row. It does **not**
include the drop shadow, the background overlay (smoke layer), the
desktop window frame, or the desktop title bar. macOS sheets are
fully AppKit-managed and expose no dimension constraints.

Button order convention differs significantly across platforms.
macOS primary action = rightmost. Windows primary = leftmost. KDE:
Help/Reset left-aligned, then stretch, then OK/Apply/Cancel right-aligned
(OK left of Cancel). GNOME: cancel left, affirmative right.
Modern WinUI 3 ContentDialog preserves this convention: PrimaryButton
(the "do it" action) is leftmost; CloseButton (the safe action) is
rightmost (Microsoft WinUI 3 Dialog controls, ms.date 2025-08-05,
verified 2026-04-20).

Dialog internal spacing (title-to-body, body-to-buttons) is baked into
each platform's dialog template, not exposed as a single adjustable gap.
macOS: AppKit-managed (not queryable). Windows: ContentDialog XAML
template controls layout. KDE: `Layout_DefaultSpacing` = 6 between
elements. GNOME: `.message-area` padding-top=32, `.response-area`
padding-top=12 / padding-bottom=24 (per `_message-dialog.scss`).

macOS uses radiating fins, not a stroke ring. KDE and GNOME use a
rotating `process-working-symbolic` icon.

macOS has a first-class segmented control, and so has libadwaita ≥ 1.7, `AdwToggleGroup` ("Since: 1.7", [adw-toggle-group.c:114](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/a5030fc692855c62a0014ea784d3a80ce30f926f/src/adw-toggle-group.c#L114)).
`NSSegmentedControl` styles: `.automatic`, `.rounded`, `.roundRect`, `.texturedRounded`,
`.capsule`, `.texturedSquare`, `.smallSquare`, `.separated`.

macOS's counterpart is the box (`NSBox`, SwiftUI `GroupBox`), whose default look Apple does not publish;
KDE's is Kirigami's `Card`. WinUI3 has card color resources but no Card control (open proposal #6543).
GNOME defines `.card` CSS class used by `list.boxed-list`.

[Common_themeresources_any.xaml:209, :5](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Common_themeresources_any.xaml#L209)), unchanged on hover (Expander_themeresources.xaml:43, :19) | **(Breeze src)** `arrowColor(palette, WindowText)` = `KColorUtils::mix(WindowText, Window, arrowShade)`, arrowShade 0.15 ([breezehelper.cpp:206-219, :38](https://github.com/KDE/breeze/blob/f0b1d7534aa2356d7336241d0c7051522e8a6b68/kstyle/breezehelper.cpp#L206-L219)), the arrow primitive's colour outside tab bars and tool buttons ([breezestyle.cpp:4466-4470](https://github.com/KDE/breeze/blob/f0b1d7534aa2356d7336241d0c7051522e8a6b68/kstyle/breezestyle.cpp#L4466-L4470)) — Breeze #424447, Breeze Dark #dbdbdc (computed with KF 6.30.0 `KColorUtils::mix`); hovered, `hoverColor()` #3daee9 (:4420-4421) | **(Adwaita CSS)** the row's `currentColor` at `--dim-opacity` 55 %: `image.expander-row-arrow { @extend .dimmed }` ([_lists.scss:389-390](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/stylesheet/widgets/_lists.scss#L389-L390); [_labels.scss:13-15](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/stylesheet/widgets/_labels.scss#L13-L15); [_colors.scss:319](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/stylesheet/_colors.scss#L319)) | **(none)** |

### 2.28 Link
| Property      | macOS                    | Windows                            | KDE                              | GNOME                         | Material |

| `font`        | ← `defaults.font`    | ← `defaults.font`               | ← `defaults.font`            | ← `defaults.font`           | **(none)** — M3 has no link component or link colour role. `comp.text-button` (`primary` label, 12 side padding) is the nearest control ([v0_192/_md-comp-text-button.scss:46](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-comp-text-button.scss#L46)). |
| `font.color`  | `linkColor` · macOS 26: same ([probe 26 :313](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-26/probe.toml#L313), :387) | **(Fluent)** `HyperlinkForeground` = `AccentTextFillColorPrimaryBrush` (§2.1.3 `link_color`) | `ForegroundLink`              | `var(--accent-color)`         | **(none)** |

| `background_color`  | **(none)** — inline · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value | inline `Hyperlink`: none — the class has no `Background` property (`hover_background` below), so transparent, the preset's value (the model's link is inline text); **(Fluent)** `HyperlinkButton`: `HyperlinkButtonBackground` = `SubtleFillColorTransparentBrush` #00FFFFFF ([HyperlinkButton_themeresources.xaml:24, :9](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/HyperlinkButton_themeresources.xaml#L24); Common_themeresources_any.xaml:229, :25) | **(none)** — inline      | **(none)** — inline           | **(none)** |
| `hover_background`    | **(none)** · macOS 26: ❓ — no source gives its macOS 26 value | inline `Hyperlink`: none — the class has no `Background` property ([Hyperlink](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/windows-app-sdk/api/winrt/microsoft.ui.xaml.documents.hyperlink), updated 2026-07-28) and changes only its foreground per state ([Hyperlink.cpp:609-635](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/dxaml/xcp/core/text/TextBlock/Hyperlink.cpp#L609-L635)), so transparent, the preset's value (the model's link is inline text); **(Fluent)** `HyperlinkButton`: `HyperlinkButtonBackgroundPointerOver` = `SubtleFillColorSecondaryBrush` — L #09000000, D #0FFFFFFF ([HyperlinkButton_themeresources.xaml:25, :10](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/HyperlinkButton_themeresources.xaml#L25); Common_themeresources_any.xaml:230, :26), over its transparent rest fill (:24, :9) | **(none)**                   | **(none)**                    | **(none)** |
| `hover_text_color` | ❓ — `linkColor` has no hover variant, and AppKit publishes none · macOS 26: ❓ — the same | **(Fluent)** inline `Hyperlink`: `HyperlinkForegroundPointerOver` = `AccentTextFillColorSecondaryBrush` ([Hyperlink_themeresources.xaml:16, :6](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Hyperlink_themeresources.xaml#L16); looked up in the pointer-over state, [Hyperlink.cpp:626-632](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/dxaml/xcp/core/text/TextBlock/Hyperlink.cpp#L626-L632)) — L `SystemAccentColorDark3`, D `SystemAccentColorLight3` ([Common_themeresources_any.xaml:298, :94](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Common_themeresources_any.xaml#L298)): #001a68 / #99ebff for the default accent #0078d4 on Windows 11 25H2 ([probe.toml:753, :759](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-windows-11-arm/probe.toml#L753)) | ← `font.color`: Kirigami's `LinkButton` is `disabledColor`, `pressedColor` or `normalColor`, nothing on hover ([templates/LinkButton.qml:87-93](https://invent.kde.org/frameworks/kirigami/-/blob/8319acc7dd1149664147ec1e22c39ae6143ed752/src/templates/LinkButton.qml#L87-L93)) | **(Adwaita CSS)** `HSL(from $link_color h calc(s * 1.1) calc(l * 1.1))`: the accent 10 % more saturated and lighter ([_links.scss:11-13](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/stylesheet/widgets/_links.scss#L11-L13); `$link_color` = `var(--accent-color)`, [_colors.scss:265](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/stylesheet/_colors.scss#L265)) | **(none)** — M3 has no link (`font` above) |

| `disabled_text_color` | ❓ — not published · macOS 26: ❓ — the same | inline `Hyperlink`: **(none)** — its foreground has three states, normal, pointer-over and pressed ([Hyperlink.cpp:607-644](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/dxaml/xcp/core/text/TextBlock/Hyperlink.cpp#L607-L644)); **(Fluent)** `HyperlinkButton`: `HyperlinkButtonForegroundDisabled` = `AccentTextFillColorDisabledBrush` — L #5C000000, D #5DFFFFFF ([HyperlinkButton_themeresources.xaml:23, :8](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/HyperlinkButton_themeresources.xaml#L23); [Common_themeresources_any.xaml:214, :10](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Common_themeresources_any.xaml#L214)) | **(KF6 src)** `disabledColor` = `Kirigami.Theme.textColor` ([templates/LinkButton.qml:68-73](https://invent.kde.org/frameworks/kirigami/-/blob/8319acc7dd1149664147ec1e22c39ae6143ed752/src/templates/LinkButton.qml#L68-L73)), the text colour, not a link colour, from the Disabled group a disabled item takes ([plasmadesktoptheme.cpp:267-274, :289](https://invent.kde.org/frameworks/qqc2-desktop-style/-/blob/v6.31.0/kirigami-plasmadesktop-integration/plasmadesktoptheme.cpp#L267-L289)) — in the Window set #a0a1a3 / #686a6c (§2.1.6) | **(Adwaita CSS)** `color-mix(in srgb, currentColor var(--disabled-opacity), transparent)`: the link colour at 50 % ([_links.scss:19](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/stylesheet/widgets/_links.scss#L19)), inside a label that `label:disabled` also fades ([_labels.scss:8-10](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/stylesheet/widgets/_labels.scss#L8-L10)) | **(none)** |

Windows `HyperlinkButton` is a full button control with hover/press
states. Other platforms style links as inline text with underline.

### 2.29 Text Area (multi-line)

A multi-line text field is framed, filled and lettered as the single-line
field (§2.4) on every platform; its padding alone differs. The model keeps it
in `text_area.border`, whose frame inherits `input.border`.

| Property          | macOS                       | Windows                  | KDE                          | GNOME                        | Material |
|-------------------|-----------------------------|--------------------------|------------------------------|------------------------------|---|
| `border.padding_horizontal` | **(none)** — an NSTextView sets each line `lineFragmentPadding` in from its text container's edges ("The default value for this property is `5.0`", [NSTextContainer.lineFragmentPadding](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nstextcontainer/linefragmentpadding)), and the container `textContainerInset` in from the view's, a default its page does not state ([NSTextView.textContainerInset](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nstextview/textcontainerinset)) · macOS 26: same ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nstextcontainer/linefragmentpadding.json), read 2026-10-11) | WinUI3: 10 left / 6 right — a multi-line field is the same TextBox (`AcceptsReturn`), padded `TextControlThemePadding` = 10,5,6,6 ([Common_themeresources.xaml:12](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Common_themeresources.xaml#L12); [TextBox_themeresources.xaml:194](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/8463f45162149de0ec3ad7df752596893fe3e13e/controls/dev/CommonStyles/TextBox_themeresources.xaml#L194)) inside its border | **(Breeze src)** 5 inside the frame line — a QTextEdit is a scroll area, which Breeze frames `Frame_FrameWidth` = 2 wide when it shares a layout with spacing (`PM_DefaultFrameWidth`, [breezestyle.cpp:659-720](https://github.com/KDE/breeze/blob/f0b1d7534aa2356d7336241d0c7051522e8a6b68/kstyle/breezestyle.cpp#L659-L720); [breezemetrics.h:41](https://github.com/KDE/breeze/blob/f0b1d7534aa2356d7336241d0c7051522e8a6b68/kstyle/breezemetrics.h#L41)), its 1px line the outermost, and its document sets the text `documentMargin` = 4 in ([qtextdocument_p.cpp:180](https://github.com/qt/qtbase/blob/ef55f427f2c8b410d34f8a7681020a3000cf6866/src/gui/text/qtextdocument_p.cpp#L180)): 2 + 4 − 1. **(measured)** 2026-09-28, a Breeze QTextEdit (`kdialog --textinputbox`, KWin, scale 1.0, Noto Sans 10pt): the text starts 6px from the frame's outer left edge | **(GTK)** 0 — `textview` sets no padding ([_views.scss:20-44](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/libadwaita/-/blob/1.10.0/src/stylesheet/widgets/_views.scss#L20-L44)), and GtkTextView's `left-margin` and `right-margin` default to 0 ([gtktextview.c:1053-1070](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/gtk/-/blob/4.24.1/gtk/gtktextview.c#L1053-L1070)) | **(derived)** 15 — material-web 16 from the field's edge ([mw/tokens/_md-comp-outlined-text-field.scss:139-140](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/_md-comp-outlined-text-field.scss#L139-L140)), less the 1 outline laid over it, as the single-line field's (§2.4) |
| `border.padding_vertical` | **(none)** — the same `textContainerInset`, whose default is not stated · macOS 26: same ([docs](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nstextview/textcontainerinset.json), read 2026-10-11) | WinUI3: 5 top / 6 bottom — the same `TextControlThemePadding` | **(Breeze src)** 5 — the same 2 + 4 − 1. **(measured)** the same field: its first 18px line box starts 6px below the frame's outer top edge | **(GTK)** 0 — `top-margin` and `bottom-margin` default to 0 (gtktextview.c:1083-1100) | **(derived)** 15 — material-web 16 from the field's edge ([mw/tokens/_md-comp-outlined-text-field.scss:141-142](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/cbd34a8921915af94d5ef65c2a69eece41d5b4f3/tokens/_md-comp-outlined-text-field.scss#L141-L142)), less the 1 outline laid over it |
| `border.color`    | ← `input.border.color`      | ← `input.border.color`   | ← `input.border.color`       | ← `input.border.color`       | ← `input.border.color` (`outline`) |
| `border.line_width` | ← `input.border.line_width` | ← `input.border.line_width` | ← `input.border.line_width` | ← `input.border.line_width` | ← `input.border.line_width` (1) |
| `border.corner_radius` | ← `input.border.corner_radius` | ← `input.border.corner_radius` | ← `input.border.corner_radius` | ← `input.border.corner_radius` | ← `input.border.corner_radius` (4) |
| `border.shadow_enabled` | ← `input.border.shadow_enabled` | ← `input.border.shadow_enabled` | ← `input.border.shadow_enabled` | ← `input.border.shadow_enabled` | ← `input.border.shadow_enabled` (false) |

---

## Appendix: Verification Sources (2026-03-24)

Every value in this document was cross-checked against internet sources.
Values marked ✅ are confirmed, ❓ uncertain/unverifiable.
Below are the authoritative sources used, organized by platform.

### macOS

| What was verified | Source |
|---|---|
| NSFont class methods exist and roles are correct | [NSFont — Apple Developer Documentation](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsfont) |
| `systemFontSize`=13, `smallSystemFontSize`=11, `labelFontSize`=10 | [Monkeybread NSFont reference](https://www.monkeybreadsoftware.net/cocoa-nsfontmbs-shared-method.shtml) — explicitly states the three values |
| `.body`=13pt confirmed | [WWDC 2020 Session 10175 "The details of UI typography"](https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2020/10175/) — "13 pt" for body on macOS |
| All TextStyle sizes and weights | ✅ [Apple HIG Typography Specifications](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/typography) — macOS built-in text styles table accessible via HIG JSON API (`developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/design/human-interface-guidelines/typography.json`). All sizes confirmed: `.largeTitle`=26pt, `.caption1`=10pt, `.caption2`=10pt. Key weight corrections: `.headline` is **Bold (700)** on macOS (not SemiBold — iOS differs); `.caption2` is **Medium (500)** (not Regular). WWDC 2020 Session 10175 discusses `.body`+bold→SemiBold, not `.headline` itself. Third-party implementations (ViewKit, shaps80) predate the macOS 11 TextStyle API and have inaccurate values. |
| macOS does not support Dynamic Type | [WWDC 2020 Session 10175](https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2020/10175/) — explicitly states "Although there is no Dynamic Type support" for macOS |
| `menuBarFontOfSize:` weight | ❓ API exists ([Apple docs](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsfont/menubarfont(ofsize:))) but weight is not documented; no evidence of SemiBold — likely Regular like other font methods; needs verification on Mac hardware |
| All NSColor semantic methods exist | Individual Apple doc pages — e.g. [controlAccentColor](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nscolor/3000782-controlaccentcolor) (macOS 10.14, introduced with Dark Mode in [WWDC 2018 Session 210](https://asciiwwdc.com/2018/sessions/210)), [labelColor](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nscolor/1534657-labelcolor), etc. |
| Text insertion point APIs: `NSTextView.insertionPointColor` (old), `NSColor.textInsertionPointColor` (macOS 14+), `NSTextInsertionIndicator` (macOS 14+) | ✅ `NSColor.textInsertionPointColor` is a type property introduced in macOS 14.0 per [Apple docs JSON](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/appkit/nscolor/textinsertionpointcolor.json) (`"introducedAt": "14.0"`, `"roleHeading": "Type Property"`). Note: [martinhoeller NSColor catalog dump (14.4)](https://gist.github.com/martinhoeller/38509f37d42814526a9aecbb24928f46) does not list it because it only catalogs `NSColorType.catalog` entries, not all class properties. `NSTextInsertionIndicator`: [Apple docs](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nstextinsertionindicator) |
| `systemTealColor` = macOS 10.12 | Apple SDK headers: `API_AVAILABLE(macos(10.12))` in [NSColor.h (10.15 SDK)](https://github.com/phracker/MacOSX-SDKs/blob/master/MacOSX10.15.sdk/System/Library/Frameworks/AppKit.framework/Versions/C/Headers/NSColor.h); class-dump from [w0lfschild/macOS_headers](https://github.com/w0lfschild/macOS_headers/blob/master/macOS/Frameworks/AppKit/1643.10.101/NSColor.h) confirms runtime symbol pre-10.15. WWDC 2019 said "new" because the header declaration first shipped in 10.15 SDK, but runtime symbol existed since 10.12. |
| `systemIndigoColor` = macOS 10.15 | Same SDK headers: `API_AVAILABLE(macos(10.15))`. [WWDC 2019 Session 210](https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2019/210/?time=754) introduces indigo as genuinely new. |
| `systemCyanColor` = macOS 12 | ✅ [Apple docs](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nscolor/systemcyan) confirms macOS 12.0; Apple docs JSON correctly shows `"introducedAt":"12.0"`; no class-dump evidence of pre-12 existence (unlike teal/mint which existed at runtime since 10.12) |
| `systemMintColor` = macOS 10.12 | ✅ Same pattern as `systemTealColor`: runtime symbol present in [AppKit 1504 class-dump](https://github.com/w0lfschild/macOS_headers/blob/master/macOS/Frameworks/AppKit/1504.82.104/NSColor.h) (macOS 10.12); absent from [AppKit 1348](https://github.com/w0lfschild/macOS_headers/blob/master/macOS/Frameworks/AppKit/1348.17/NSColor.h) (macOS 10.10). SDK header first appeared in macOS 12.0 SDK ([codeworkshop diff](http://codeworkshop.net/objc-diff/sdkdiffs/macos/12.0/AppKit.html)) with `API_AVAILABLE(macos(10.12))`. Apple docs JSON `introducedAt: "10.12"` is correct, not a bug. `@available(macOS 12.0, *)` guards in Swift code are overly conservative. |
| `performAsCurrentDrawingAppearance` | [Apple docs](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsappearance/3674525-performascurrentdrawingappearance) — macOS 11.0 |
| `colorUsingColorSpace:` for P3→sRGB | [Apple docs](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nscolor/usingcolorspace(_:)) |
| Window corner radius = 10px | Multiple community sources confirm 10pt through Sequoia. macOS Tahoe (26) uses variable radii per window style: [macos-corner-fix](https://github.com/m4rkw/macos-corner-fix) confirms 16pt (title-bar-only); toolbar window radii: sources disagree — [Zed discussion #38233](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/discussions/38233) reports ~26pt (from WWDC25 screenshot); [Podfeet/Steve Harris](https://www.podfeet.com/blog/2025/10/rounded-screenshots-shell-script/) measured 50px at 2× = ~25pt; [VS Code PR #270236](https://github.com/microsoft/vscode/pull/270236) suggests 20pt (compact) / 24pt (standard); [lapcatsoftware](https://lapcatsoftware.com/articles/2026/3/1.html) confirms variable radii qualitatively; [alt-tab-macos #4985](https://github.com/lwouis/alt-tab-macos/issues/4985) notes "4 or 5" distinct radii; [WebKit commit 643493b](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/commit/643493bea2f9824959ebb9824bfb011aedf7498c) reads radii dynamically via private `_cornerConfiguration` SPI (macOS 26.1+); no public API exists. |
| NSTableView rowHeight = 24pt (macOS 11+) | ✅ Changed from 17pt in Big Sur. [lapcatsoftware "BS AppKit notes"](https://lapcatsoftware.com/articles/BSAppKit.html) and [AppKit Release Notes for macOS 11](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/macos-release-notes/appkit-release-notes-for-macos-11) confirm. |
| Control corner radius = 5px | ❓ WebKit [`RenderThemeMac.mm:1351`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/de320ba3ff90229f983988d42d4b0f83315459da/Source/WebCore/rendering/mac/RenderThemeMac.mm#L1351): `const int baseBorderRadius = 5`, for CSS-styled popup/menu-list buttons only; no AppKit source, and the OS probe reads no radius |
| NSTextField intrinsic height = 22px | ✅ WebKit `RenderThemeMac.mm` search field sizes: regular=22, small=19, mini=17, large=30 |
| NSSwitch intrinsic size = 38×22, thumb ~18px | ✅ WebKit `RenderThemeMac.mm`: `switchSizes()` = {38,22} regular, {32,18} small, {26,15} mini. Thumb: WebKit [`SwitchThumbMac.mm`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/main/Source/WebCore/platform/graphics/mac/controls/SwitchThumbMac.mm): bounding box = track height (22px); visual CoreUI knob ~18px inside. |
| NSSplitView divider = 9px | ✅ The OS probe, macOS 14.8.9 to 27.0.1: `dividerThickness` 9 (`.thick`, the default style), 1 (`.thin`), 10 (`.paneSplitter`) ([probe 14 :488-495](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L488-L495)). GNUstep's [`NSSplitView.m`](https://github.com/gnustep/libs-gui/blob/master/Source/NSSplitView.m) gives 6, which AppKit does not |
| NSPopUpButton intrinsic height = 21px | ✅ WebKit `RenderThemeMac.mm`: `popupButtonSizes()` = {0,21} regular, {0,18} small, {0,15} mini, {0,24} large. Previously listed as 26px — that value is the right-padding (arrow area width), not height. |
| Spinning progress 32/16px | ✅ `NSProgressIndicator.sizeToFit` by `controlSize`: regular=32, small=16, mini=10. [Apple sizeToFit docs](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsprogressindicator/1501144-sizetofit) |
| NSSlider track height = 5px | ✅ WebKit [`SliderTrackMac.mm`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/main/Source/WebCore/platform/graphics/mac/controls/SliderTrackMac.mm): `sliderTrackWidth = 5` (previously listed as 4px) |
| NSSlider thumb = 20px (measured) vs WebKit 17px | ✅ The OS probe's render, macOS 14.8.9: a disk 40 px across at 2× ([render](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/renders/aqua/slider-regular@2x.png)); `knobThickness` returns 24, the knob's rect ([probe 14 :1423](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1423)), and WebKit's `sliderThumbThickness = 17` carries a FIXME that it should come from AppKit ([`RenderThemeMac.mm:1650-1652`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/de320ba3ff90229f983988d42d4b0f83315459da/Source/WebCore/rendering/mac/RenderThemeMac.mm#L1650-L1652)) |
| NSButton (checkbox) indicator = 14px | ❓ WebKit [`ToggleButtonMac.mm`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/main/Source/WebCore/platform/graphics/mac/controls/ToggleButtonMac.mm): regular={14,14}; Gecko [`nsNativeThemeCocoa.mm`](https://github.com/mozilla/gecko-dev/blob/master/widget/cocoa/nsNativeThemeCocoa.mm): native={16,16} — engines disagree |
| NSDisclosureButton cell = 21×21px | Gecko `nsNativeThemeCocoa.mm`: `kDisclosureButtonSize = {21, 21}` — visible triangle (~13px measured) is a subset of the cell |
| Overlay scrollbar thumb ~7px (idle) | ✅ Gecko [`ScrollbarDrawingCocoa.cpp`](https://searchfox.org/mozilla-central/source/widget/ScrollbarDrawingCocoa.cpp): overlay non-hovered thumb = 7px (8px base − 1px overlay adjustment), hovered = 11px, within a 16px overlay track; Chromium [`native_theme_mac.mm`](https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/master/ui/native_theme/native_theme_mac.mm): `GetThumbMinSize()` = {6,18} (6px minimum thumb width); Chromium [`overlay_scrollbar_constants.h`](https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/master/ui/native_theme/overlay_scrollbar_constants.h): `kOverlayScrollbarThumbWidthPressed` = 10px, idle scale = 0.4; WebKit [`ScrollbarThemeMac.mm`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/main/Source/WebCore/platform/mac/ScrollbarThemeMac.mm) delegates to native `NSScrollerImp` (dynamic, no hardcoded value). Two engines agree on ~6–7px idle thumb width. |
| Other geometry/widget metrics (0.5px, padding values, etc.) | ❓ Apple does not publish these. All are measured values. Legacy scrollbar width = ✅ 15px, `scrollerWidth(for: .regular, scrollerStyle: .legacy)` on macOS 14.8.9 ([probe 14 :481](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L481)); a [developer measurement](https://gist.github.com/martynchamberlin/6aaf8a45b36907e9f1e21a28889f6b0a) gave 16. Disabled state uses `disabledControlTextColor` (alpha ≈0.247, confirmed across macOS Catalina–Monterey by [andrejilderda gist](https://gist.github.com/andrejilderda/8677c565cddc969e6aae7df48622d47c) and [zrzka gist](https://gist.github.com/zrzka/7836c8339e0141601aa4a02a3f2e04c6)), not a global opacity multiplier. |
| Focus ring width = 3px | ✅ Confirmed via WebKit SPI: `UIFocusRingStyle.borderThickness = 3` ([WebKit outline-style:auto commit](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/commit/c3770c7b04d216f822e3a4308c43b01ec0e7afed)); [Mozilla Bug 53927](https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=53927) (Mac OS 9 era, 2px) is obsolete. Modern focus ring is a diffuse glow — 3px is the settled border thickness. |
| NSButton height = 20px | ✅ The OS probe, macOS 14.8.9: `intrinsicContentSize` 61 × 20 ([probe 14 :1057](evidence/os-probe/2026-10-11-run-38129819806/os-probe-macos-14/probe.toml#L1057)); the 22 of developer discussions is not what AppKit reports |
| NSButton horizontal padding ~8px | ❓ WebKit [`RenderThemeMac.mm`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/main/Source/WebCore/rendering/mac/RenderThemeMac.mm) `controlPadding(PushButton)` = 8px horizontal; comment says "AppKit wants to use 11px for mini buttons." Gecko `nsNativeThemeCocoa.mm` `pushButtonSettings` margins `{0,5,2,5}` are *external* outsets (focus ring/chrome), not content padding; Gecko CSS `<button>` uses `padding-inline: 4px`. Legacy HIG 12px is inter-button *spacing*. Native bezel internal padding is not directly queryable. |
| NSStackView default spacing = 8pt | [Apple docs NSStackView.spacing](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsstackview/spacing) — "default value is 8.0 points" |
| IB standard spacing = 8pt between siblings, 20pt to superview | [Auto Layout Guide](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/UserExperience/Conceptual/AutolayoutPG/WorkingwithSimpleConstraints.html) |
| Label-to-control = 8pt (regular) | Legacy Apple HIG — 8px regular, 6px small, 5px mini |
| Toolbar regular=32, small=24 | [NSToolbar.SizeMode docs](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nstoolbar/sizemode) (deprecated) |
| Sidebar icon sizes 16/20/24 (macOS 11+) | ✅ Apple HIG Sidebars page (macOS section, archived 2022 via Wayback Machine) documented full metrics table: Small=16×16px (row 24pt), Medium=20×20px (row 28pt), Large=24×24px (row 32pt). Table removed from current HIG ~2024. Pre-Big Sur legacy sizes were 16/18/32 (from CoreTypes.bundle). |
| Menu bar extra icon = 16pt | [Bjango guide](https://bjango.com/articles/designingmenubarextras/) — community best-practice, not official |
| Accessibility APIs | All confirmed: [reduceMotion](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace/accessibilitydisplayshouldreducemotion) (10.12), [reduceTransparency](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace/accessibilitydisplayshouldreducetransparency) (10.10), [increaseContrast](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace/accessibilitydisplayshouldincreasecontrast) (10.10), [differentiateWithoutColor](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace/accessibilitydisplayshoulddifferentiatewithoutcolor) (10.10) |
| `NSFont.preferredFont(forTextStyle:)` does NOT auto-scale | ✅ WWDC 2020 Session 10175 explicitly states macOS has no Dynamic Type; sizes are fixed. macOS 14 (Sonoma) added limited "Text Size" in Accessibility settings (few Apple apps only), but `preferredFont(forTextStyle:)` still returns fixed sizes. |
| NSScroller `.overlay`/`.legacy` | [NSScroller.preferredScrollerStyle](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsscroller/preferredscrollerstyle) — macOS 10.7 |
| NSSwitch introduced macOS 10.15 | [NSSwitch docs](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsswitch) + WWDC 2019 Session 210 |
| "Between related controls = 8pt" oversimplified | Legacy HIG specifies 12px for regular push buttons, 8px only for mini/icon buttons |
| `+toolTipsFontOfSize:` default size | ❓ [Apple API docs](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsfont/1527704-tooltipsfontofsize) do not state default size; [Leopard-era Apple HIG](https://leopard-adc.pepas.com/documentation/UserExperience/Conceptual/AppleHIGuidelines/XHIGText/XHIGText.html) states "The small system font (11 point) is the default font for help tags" (Apple's term for tooltips), strongly supporting 11pt; [Cocotron NSFont.m](https://github.com/berkus/cocotron/blob/master/AppKit/NSFont.m) defaults to 10pt; [GNUstep NSFont.m](https://github.com/gnustep/libs-gui/blob/master/Source/NSFont.m) defaults to 12pt; open-source impls disagree with each other and with the HIG |
| NSProgressIndicator visual bar vs control frame | ❓ bar height 6px is the visual track; `NSProgressIndicatorPreferredThickness`=14px deprecated (Apple: "do not accurately represent the geometry"); WebKit [`ProgressBarMac.mm`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/main/Source/WebCore/platform/graphics/mac/controls/ProgressBarMac.mm) cell sizes: regular=20px, small=12px, mini=12px (frame height, not visual bar); Chromium `LayoutThemeMac.mm` agrees (20, 12, 12); [GRProgressIndicator](https://github.com/insidegui/GRProgressIndicator) confirms visual bar is drawn smaller than frame. Visual track height is rendered by CoreUI — no engine exposes the exact value. |

Breeze sets DesktopDefault=48 (C++ fallback=32), PanelDefault=48 (matches C++ fallback; was 32 until KF5 v5.34.0) |

| forceFontDPI in kcmfonts (no `rc`: KConfig adds it only to an empty name, [kconfig.cpp:649, :652-678](https://invent.kde.org/frameworks/kconfig/-/blob/v6.31.0/src/core/kconfig.cpp#L652-L678), v6.31.0) | Historically X11-only (Plasma 5 guarded UI with `#if HAVE_X11`); [commit f97930a](https://github.com/KDE/plasma-desktop/commit/f97930a8cc3b620a2b780ebf0df685ba36188cfa) removed X11 guard; [issue #62](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-desktop/-/issues/62) approved removing for Wayland. In Plasma 6.7: [fonts KCM main.qml](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/blob/v6.7.5/kcms/fonts/ui/main.qml#L424-L426) lines 424-426 hide the UI on Wayland (`visible: Qt.platform.pluginName === "xcb"`); [plasma6.0-remove-dpi-settings.cpp](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/blob/v6.7.5/kcms/fonts/kconf_update/plasma6.0-remove-dpi-settings.cpp#L25) deletes `forceFontDPIWayland` on upgrade. On X11 the key works if set by hand; on Wayland krdb ignores it ([krdb.cpp:333-341](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/blob/v6.7.5/kcms/krdb/krdb.cpp#L333-L341)). Plasma 6.8 removes the key and its reader ([c675077b](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/commit/c675077b8f7371eb65bfa73c20d86a50828b13fc), [fe367323](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/commit/fe367323b30bfa239243b2164d75faacb1be15e7); checked at Beta 2, v6.7.91). |
| AnimationDurationFactor in kdeglobals [KDE], 0=disabled | ✅ Confirmed per [kwin.kcfg](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/kwin/-/blob/v6.7.5/src/kwin.kcfg#L313-L317) (`<min>0</min>`, Plasma 6.7.5); 0 yields `std::max(actualTime, 1)`, `actualTime = defaultTime × animationTimeFactor()`, = 1ms per [effect.cpp:447-452](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/kwin/-/blob/v6.7.5/src/effect/effect.cpp#L447-L452) — effectively instant, not literally zero; [Phabricator D28651](https://phabricator.kde.org/D28651), [bug 431259](https://bugs.kde.org/show_bug.cgi?id=431259) |
| Breeze PanelDefault=48 (matches C++ fallback) | [breeze-icons commonthemeinfo.theme.in:16](https://invent.kde.org/frameworks/breeze-icons/-/blob/v6.31.0/icons/commonthemeinfo.theme.in#L16) — `PanelDefault=48`; C++ fallback in [kicontheme.cpp:165](https://invent.kde.org/frameworks/kiconthemes/-/blob/v6.31.0/src/kicontheme.cpp#L165) is also 48 (KF 6.31.0). Was 32 until KF5 v5.34.0 (~2017), changed to 48 in later versions. |
| Dialog button spacing = Layout_DefaultSpacing = 6 | QDialogButtonBox uses `PM_LayoutHorizontalSpacing` → Breeze returns `Layout_DefaultSpacing` = 6 per [breezemetrics.h:47](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/breeze/-/blob/v6.7.5/kstyle/breezemetrics.h#L47) (Breeze v6.7.5). `Button_ItemSpacing`=4 ([:76](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/breeze/-/blob/v6.7.5/kstyle/breezemetrics.h#L76)) is icon-to-label gap inside a single button, not inter-button spacing. |
| `[General] AccentColor` propagates to `DecorationFocus` | [colorsapplicator.cpp:129-183](https://invent.kde.org/plasma/plasma-workspace/-/blob/v6.7.5/kcms/colors/colorsapplicator.cpp#L129-L183) (Plasma 6.7.5) — reads `AccentColor` from `[General]`; applies to `ForegroundActive`, `ForegroundLink`, `DecorationFocus`, `DecorationHover` across color groups |
